# Dream Stale-Memory Reconciliation

**Plan ID:** dream-stale-memory-reconciliation

## Overview
`kind: framing`

Task #18430 investigated why `memory_dream` never reconciled stale memory `f0652bc1` ("TDD sandwich implemented in execute_expansion()", 2026-03-28, access_count>100) against decision memory `ee3b9e52` (#15008, 2026-05-22, "remove legacy TEST/IMPL/REF sandwich generation entirely"). Root cause: the dream reviewed the stale memory nightly and always judged `keep` because (1) candidates are judged independently against a truth digest — no cross-memory comparison exists anywhere; (2) the truth digest carries only 5 hardcoded platform facts for the daemon project, so code-behavior claims are unfalsifiable; (3) the prompt treats high `access_count` as keep evidence, so wrong-but-popular memories self-reinforce; (4) the two memories are semantically distant (~0.169 cosine), so similarity-based pairing (crossrefs ≥0.66, hybrid recall with temporal decay) can never link them — only keyword/BM25 overlap on distinctive lexemes ("sandwich", TEST/IMPL/REF) can. This plan gives the dream planner per-candidate related-newer-memory evidence, removes meaning-destroying content truncation, adds write-time supersession, blends the project truth digest, replaces all `gpt-5.5` model references with the gpt-5.6 family (sol/terra/luna per tier), and removes the static context-window table in favor of override/provider/registry resolution.

## Constraints
`kind: framing`

- No content truncation anywhere in dream prompt rendering: a memory cut mid-sentence can invert its meaning, and `refresh` writes LLM-authored replacement content from what was rendered — truncation there is silent data loss. Bounds must be structural (whole-item caps, batch splitting), never content-cutting.
- No new per-candidate LLM or embedding calls in retrieval: reuse Postgres BM25 keyword search and stored Qdrant vectors only.
- Evidence retrieval must not apply temporal decay and must never fail or stall a sweep (best-effort, log-and-continue).
- Dream action vocabulary stays `keep/delete/refresh/review/promote`; `supersede`/`merge` stay out of `_VALID_ACTIONS` (the dream-apply `_supersede` handler duplicates content and hard-deletes — deliberately retired).
- `allow_unattended_mutations` default stays `False` in code (#16163 stands for other installs); only Josh's local config flips it.
- Model replacement scope is ALL `gpt-5.5` references in functional code, config, templates, tests, doc-comments, user-facing guides, and the web UI (Josh's directive, 2026-07-18): both feature chains, the providers.py catalog entry (swap explicitly authorized for this deliberate migration, overriding the never-delete-catalog-models default from the 71319547f/#17775 review lesson), Rust doc comments and all test fixtures. Non-goals: historical artifacts — `docs/evidence/`, completed plans, `.gobby/memories.jsonl`, wiki pages — record experiments that genuinely ran on gpt-5.5 and are not rewritten.
- gpt-5.6 family mapping for every remaining functional `gpt-5.5` reference (R2 F5): low/mini-tier usages (including `gpt-5.5-mini`) → `gpt-5.6-luna`; mid-tier → `gpt-5.6-terra`; high/xhigh-tier → `gpt-5.6-sol` (keeping any stated reasoning effort, `xhigh` where present); tier-neutral fixtures and docs mirror the production path they exercise.
- DO NOT create backward compatibility; 0.5.0 is unshipped.

P0 also builds the bounded async hub connection surface (0.3) that every later DB-touching drain/cleanup path consumes (R15 F1/F2).

### 0.3 Bounded async hub connection surface [category: code] (depends: 0.2)
`kind: deliverable`

Target: `src/gobby/storage/hub/protocol.py`, `src/gobby/storage/hub/postgres.py`, `src/gobby/storage/hub/async_ops.py` (new), `tests/storage/hub/test_async_ops.py` (new), `tests/storage/hub/test_protocol_contract.py`

**Why this exists (R15 F1, R15 F2 — root cause of four prior rounds of drain machinery)**: a synchronous psycopg worker is not locally terminatable — every escape built on `Connection.cancel_safe` needs SERVER cooperation, so a blocked, timed-out, or failed cancel request ends in a logged-and-abandoned non-daemon thread that still owns a live connection: repeated failures accumulate threads past any per-session cap, block interpreter exit (`concurrent.futures` joins its workers at shutdown), and permit DB activity after storage teardown. Async psycopg I/O removes the class: every wait is an awaitable the event loop can abandon, and closing the connection is a LOCAL operation. This section builds that surface once; 1.1's evidence channels, 2.1's supersession cleanup and visibility fences, 2.1's import primary transactions (R29 F1), and 3.4's metadata refresh write consume it instead of proving termination independently.

- **Hub conninfo**: the hub protocol and `PostgresHubDatabase` expose a read-only `conninfo: str` property (backed by the existing normalized `_conninfo`), so async consumers open their own connections from the same DSN without touching the sync pool.
- **New `src/gobby/storage/hub/async_ops.py`** — one supervisor-shaped primitive, `run_bounded_db(work, *, conninfo, deadline_seconds, statement_timeout_remaining=True, lock_timeout=False)`: opens a DEDICATED `psycopg.AsyncConnection` (`connect_timeout` drawn from the remaining monotonic deadline), begins a transaction, installs `SET LOCAL statement_timeout` (and `lock_timeout` when requested) at the post-connect remainder, runs the caller's `work(conn, remaining)` coroutine, commits — all as a CHILD task supervised under the deadline, with a named terminal slice reserved INSIDE it (R17 F1): module constant `RUN_BOUNDED_DB_CLEANUP_SLICE_SECONDS = 1.0` — every work-phase budget (connect_timeout, the SET LOCAL remainder, `work`'s `remaining`, the commit gate) is drawn against the WORK CUTOFF at `deadline_seconds` minus the slice, so cancellation, grace, and terminal escalation spend reserved time, never time past the caller's deadline. At the work cutoff (or on supervisor cancellation): cancel the child (psycopg's own cancellation handler makes a bounded server-cancel attempt — `wait()` → `_try_cancel(timeout=5.0)` in psycopg 3.3.4 — on a SEPARATE cancel connection: `_cancel_gen` opens its own `PGcancelConn` socket via `pgconn.cancel_conn()` and waits it under psycopg's fixed 5s cancel timeout before re-waiting the main socket, so cancellation ALONE is not the bound and closing the MAIN connection alone cannot wake the cancel-connection wait, R16 F1); the supervisor then observes the child with a NON-CANCELLING bounded wait for a short grace INSIDE the reserved slice (R17 F1), and if the child has not terminated, runs the TWO-STEP terminal sequence (R16 F1): HARD-CLOSE the main connection — `pgconn.finish()` is a local socket close needing no server cooperation, and a closed fd fails any main-socket wait immediately — and issue a SECOND child cancellation, which interrupts an in-flight cancel-connection wait (`_try_cancel` catches only `Exception`, and `CancelledError` is a `BaseException`, so the second cancellation propagates uncaught through `wait_conn_async` and terminates the child at once). Every possible wait site is then dead — main-socket waits fail on the closed fd, the cancel-connection wait dies to the propagating cancellation — so grace, terminal escalation, and child reap all complete inside the reserved slice and the call returns no later than the caller's ORIGINAL `deadline_seconds` (scheduler tolerance only, R17 F1), never extended by psycopg's internal cancel timeout, even when the server-side cancel request itself blocks or fails; the supervisor awaits the child under the slice's remaining monotonic budget. The uncommitted transaction dies with the socket: PostgreSQL aborts it and releases its row locks as soon as the backend observes EOF (an idle-in-transaction backend is blocked reading that socket; a lock-waiting or executing backend is additionally bounded by the installed `lock_timeout`/`statement_timeout`). No thread is ever created; there is NO abandonment path — the terminal escalation is a local close plus a propagating cancellation, neither needing server cooperation.
- **Commit phase is explicit (R16 F2)**: COMMIT is a point of no return. The supervisor checks the remaining monotonic WORK budget (against the work cutoff — the reserved terminal slice is never spent waiting on a COMMIT response, R17 F1) BEFORE submitting COMMIT — a nonpositive remainder rolls back instead, so guaranteed rollback holds for every expiry that PRECEDES COMMIT submission. Once COMMIT has been sent, an expiry, cancellation, or connection loss before the response arrives is INDETERMINATE — the server may have applied the commit and `pgconn.finish()` cannot undo it — so the call surfaces a typed indeterminate-commit outcome (distinct from the deterministic rolled-back timeout outcome) and never claims rollback. Write consumers must be idempotent or state-verifiable on retry; every write consumer in this plan is (3.4's refresh is a full delete-and-reinsert reconciled by the next cycle, 2.1's purge and fence steps are idempotent re-purgeable transactions, 2.1's import primaries are idempotent LWW upserts and tombstones whose indeterminate commits are resolved by a bounded state-verification read with a conservative mark-due policy (R29 F1), and 1.1's evidence calls are read-only, where commit outcome carries no state).
- `statement_timeout`/`lock_timeout` are retained as server-side hygiene ONLY: they bound the orphaned server-side query or lock waiter when the cancel request cannot reach the server; local termination never depends on them.
- The sync `HubDatabase` surface, pool, and every existing caller are untouched; `async_ops.py` is additive.

**Acceptance:**

- 0.3.1 - The hub protocol and PostgreSQL implementation expose the read-only `conninfo` property and agree on it. test: `tests/storage/hub/test_protocol_contract.py`.
- 0.3.2 - Termination matrix, proven once for every consumer: with (a) a blocked connect (endpoint accepts TCP, never completes the handshake), (b) a worker blocked on its FIRST `SET LOCAL` round trip (no timeout installed yet), (c) a server-side-blocked statement, (d) a row-lock acquisition blocked by a foreign holder outlasting the deadline, and (e) a cancellation whose server-cancel attempt itself fails (cancel endpoint unreachable or accepting-then-silent — the attempt runs on psycopg's SEPARATE cancel connection) — each `run_bounded_db` call returns within its ORIGINAL deadline via the two-step terminal sequence, never extended by psycopg's fixed 5-second internal cancel timeout (R16 F1), with the timing arithmetic proven explicitly (R17 F1): the work cutoff lands at `deadline_seconds` minus `RUN_BOUNDED_DB_CLEANUP_SLICE_SECONDS`, the first cancellation is issued at the cutoff, the non-cancelling grace wait and the two-step terminal escalation run inside the reserved slice, and the child is reaped and its resources accounted before the call returns — asserted with an explicit scheduler-tolerance bound on measured elapsed time — the event loop stays responsive throughout (a sentinel task completes promptly during the block), zero DB/stub activity occurs after the call returns, no thread is created at any point (thread accounting), and repeated cycles across many calls leak nothing (stable task, thread, and fd counts; interpreter exit unaffected). test: `tests/storage/hub/test_async_ops.py::test_termination_matrix`.
- 0.3.3 - Lock-release and commit-phase proof against real PostgreSQL: a `run_bounded_db` transaction holding `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` that is hard-closed mid-flight releases the row lock promptly — a second connection acquires it within the deadline margin; a committed transaction's effects persist; a transaction whose expiry precedes COMMIT submission rolls back completely; and the commit-applied/response-lost shape — a proxy forwards COMMIT to the server and drops the response — surfaces the typed indeterminate-commit outcome (never a rollback claim) while a follow-up connection observes the applied effects (R16 F2). test: `tests/storage/hub/test_async_ops.py::test_lock_release_on_hard_close`. test: `tests/storage/hub/test_async_ops.py::test_commit_phase_outcomes`.

## P1: Review-Time Contradiction Evidence
`kind: framing`

**Goal**: The dream planner sees each candidate's strongest newer related memories and can cite them as concrete obsolescence signals.

### 1.1 Add related-memory retrieval module and stored-vector search [category: code] (depends: P0, 1.2)
`kind: deliverable`

Target: `src/gobby/memory/dream/related.py` (new), `src/gobby/memory/vectorstore.py`, `src/gobby/memory/vectorstore_queries.py`, `src/gobby/memory/vectorstore_client.py`, `src/gobby/memory/vectorstore_filters.py`, `src/gobby/memory/vectorstore_maintenance.py`, `src/gobby/search/keyword.py`, `src/gobby/memory/services/keyword.py`, `src/gobby/storage/memories_crud.py`, `tests/memory/test_vectorstore.py`, `tests/memory/test_vectorstore_init.py`, `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py` (new), `tests/memory/test_manager_vectorstore.py`, `tests/search/test_unified.py`, `src/gobby/memory/services/maintenance.py`, `src/gobby/memory/facade.py`, `src/gobby/cli/memory/crud.py`, `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/memory.py`, `src/gobby/servers/routes/memory.py`, `src/gobby/servers/routes/admin/_health.py`, `tests/memory/test_maintenance_update.py`, `tests/memory/test_manager.py`, `tests/cli/test_memory_cli.py`, `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/test_memory_tools.py`, `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/test_memory.py`, `tests/servers/routes/test_memory_routes.py`, `tests/servers/routes/test_admin.py`, `tests/storage/test_memory_manager.py`, `tests/servers/test_http_endpoints.py`

New module `src/gobby/memory/dream/related.py`:

```python
class RetrievalScope:
    """Typed scope: exactly one of project_only(project_id),
    project_and_global(project_id), or global_only(). Applied IDENTICALLY
    in the keyword channel, the Qdrant filter (global_only builds an
    explicit global-payload predicate — memory_project_scope_filter(None)
    returning no filter is NOT acceptable), and hydration."""

async def gather_related_evidence(
    candidates: list[DreamCandidate],
    *,
    db: Any,                  # HubDatabase (memory_manager.db)
    vector_store: Any | None, # memory_manager._vector_store
    dream_config: Any,        # reads related_evidence_* knobs via getattr
    session: "RelatedEvidenceSession",  # caller-owned, required (R7 F1)
    scope: RetrievalScope,
    temporal_direction: Literal["newer", "older"] = "newer",
    anchor_at: datetime | None = None,
) -> list[DreamCandidate]:
    """Return candidates with `related` populated; best-effort, never raises."""
```

Sweep callers derive `scope` from `DreamRunOptions` (`global_only` → global_only; `project_id` + `include_global` → project_and_global; else project_only) and pass the caller's actual values — never pin `include_global=True`. The `session` parameter is required and caller-owned (R7 F1): `gather_related_evidence` never constructs or closes a `RelatedEvidenceSession` internally — the sweep/invocation owner creates one, passes the SAME object to every page, and closes it. `temporal_direction` selects the temporal comparison: `"newer"` (dream evidence: hit `created_at` after the anchor) or `"older"` (write-time mark-due in 2.2); both directions tested. **Anchor (R6 F5)**: the comparison reference time is `anchor_at` when provided, else `candidate.created_at` — dream sweeps omit it (review chronology unchanged); 2.2's write path passes an outcome-specific anchor because storage reactivation preserves the original `created_at`.

Internals:
- `_distinctive_terms(content: str, max_terms: int = 24) -> str` — tokenize (semantics of `sanitize_pg_search_query`, `src/gobby/search/keyword.py:391`), drop a ~50-word stopword frozenset and tokens < 3 chars, priority order: (1) task-ref digit tokens from `#(\d{3,6})`, (2) identifier-like tokens (contain `_`, ALL-CAPS, mixed alnum), (3) remaining tokens first-seen. For the f0652bc1 shape this yields a query containing `sandwich TEST IMPL REF expansion execute_expansion`, which BM25 ranks the #15008 decision memory at/near top.
- `_keyword_hits(...)` — `MemoryKeywordSearchService(db).search(query, limit=fetch_limit, ...)` (`src/gobby/memory/services/keyword.py:16`) with the explicit scope filter from the keyword scope contract below, executed as a rendered statement on the call's dedicated bounded async connection (R15 F1 — see the no-stall contract), semaphore 4.
- **Keyword scope contract (R4 F1)**: today `_filter_clauses` (`src/gobby/search/keyword.py:351`) drops the project predicate entirely when `project_id` is None and ignores `include_global` for the memories table, so a "global-only" keyword query ranks EVERY project's rows before LIMIT — hydration rejects the wrong-scope rows afterward, but more than `related_evidence_fetch_limit` higher-ranked wrong-project matches can exhaust the fetch page and starve a valid global hit (reachable false-negative). Fix at the SQL layer, before ranking/LIMIT: extend the memories-table handling in `_filter_clauses` and the `MemoryKeywordSearchService.search` signature to accept an explicit scope filter rendered into exactly three bucket predicates — `global_only` → `project_id IS NULL`, `project_only` → `project_id = %s`, `project_and_global` → `(project_id = %s OR project_id IS NULL)`. **NULL is the only global representation (R5 F1)**: `memories.project_id` is a nullable `UUID` column (`postgres_baseline_schema.sql:759`), so comparing it to an empty-string literal is a PostgreSQL type error (`invalid input syntax for type uuid`) and empty-string rows cannot exist in that column — no rendered predicate may compare `project_id` to `''`. `related.py` always passes the typed scope; existing `project_id`/`include_global` callers keep their current semantics. Hydration's scope rejection stays as defense in depth.
- `_vector_hits(...)` — one call per page to new `VectorStore.search_by_stored_vectors` with the Qdrant predicate built from `scope`; `memory_project_scope_filter(None, ...)` returning no filter is NOT acceptable for global_only. **Qdrant global payload contract (R7 F2)**: the round-5 NULL-only rule governs the PostgreSQL UUID column only — Qdrant payloads deliberately carry three legacy global representations (`memory_project_scope_filter`, `src/gobby/memory/vectorstore.py:81`, keeps `MatchValue(value="")` for legacy empty-string payloads plus `IsNullCondition` and `IsEmptyCondition`, choosing the cheap defensive match over a payload migration). The `global_only` vector predicate is therefore a `should` over exactly those three conditions with no project match; `project_only` stays a `must` project match (which excludes all three global shapes); `project_and_global` is the project match plus the three global conditions — a NULL-only vector predicate would silently drop valid legacy-payload global evidence. The scope-predicate builder lands in `src/gobby/memory/vectorstore_filters.py` — the P0-committed filter-construction module that owns `memory_project_scope_filter` after 0.1's decomposition (R30 F2). On `VectorStoreUnavailableError` degrade to keyword-only. **Vector relevance floor (F2)**: preserve `(id, score)` and drop vector hits below module constant `VECTOR_EVIDENCE_MIN_SCORE = 0.35` BEFORE fusion — Qdrant always returns nearest neighbors, so unfloored weak hits become false "related" evidence. **Calibration (R4 F5, decided in-plan — implementation carries no threshold choice)**: the two measured anchors in this corpus are the f0652bc1/ee3b9e52 pair at cosine 0.169 (semantically distant content the vector channel must treat as noise — the keyword channel owns that pair) and the `memory_crossrefs` admission floor of 0.66 (the store's high-precision link band). 0.35 sits roughly twice the measured distant-pair score and half the crossref precision floor: it rejects nearest-neighbor noise in the ~0.1–0.2 band while admitting the mid-band (0.35–0.66) semantic neighbors that crossrefs deliberately exclude. No new config knob.
- `_fuse_and_hydrate(...)` — RRF (k=60, same constant as `facade._rrf_scores`) over the two ranked id lists (vector list already floored); drop self; bulk-hydrate the page's union of hit ids with one `MemoryCrudMixin.get_memories` statement (`src/gobby/storage/memories_crud.py:314`, `visibility="active"`) **with the same scope predicate applied to hydrated rows** — the hydration statement runs on its call's dedicated bounded async connection under the same per-call timeout as the search channels (R3 F1, R15 F1); keep only rows satisfying `temporal_direction`; take `related_evidence_top_k`; `dataclasses.replace(candidate, related=...)`.
- **No-stall deadline contract (R2 F2, R3 F1, R4 F2; DB channels async-native, R15 F1)**: module constants `RELATED_EVIDENCE_PAGE_DEADLINE_SECONDS = 15.0`, `RELATED_EVIDENCE_CALL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 5.0`, and `RELATED_EVIDENCE_CHANNEL_TRIP_LIMIT = 2` — no config knobs. **No thread runs anywhere in this module (R15 F1)**: four rounds of drain machinery — per-channel tracking executors and raw-future registries (R12 F1), `cancel_safe` escalation (R14 F1), the hub `acquire_timeout` single-acquisition surface (R6 F3), and the connection-accepting service variants (R5 F2) — existed only because synchronous psycopg workers cannot be locally terminated, and every variant still ended in a logged-and-abandoned non-daemon thread when the server-side cancel request blocked or failed: abandonment accumulates threads across sessions beyond any per-session cap, blocks interpreter exit (`concurrent.futures` joins its workers at shutdown), and permits DB activity after close. All of it is deleted. The keyword and hydration channels execute their statements through 0.3's `run_bounded_db` on a DEDICATED per-call async connection opened from the hub's `conninfo` — never the sync pool, so pool starvation cannot touch evidence calls and concurrent evidence connections are bounded by the page semaphore of 4 — each call under `asyncio.timeout` on `RELATED_EVIDENCE_CALL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` and the whole page under `RELATED_EVIDENCE_PAGE_DEADLINE_SECONDS`; the vector channel stays executor-free async (R13 F2, below). Termination is 0.3's proven contract — cancellation, hard local close, and the second cancellation that interrupts psycopg's internal cancel-connection wait (R16 F1) — so there is no worker, no drain phase, and no abandonment path; `SET LOCAL statement_timeout` (installed by `run_bounded_db` at the post-connect remainder) remains server-side hygiene bounding the orphaned query when a cancel request cannot reach the server. **Rendered statements replace connection-accepting variants (R5 F2, revised R15 F1)**: today `MemoryKeywordSearchService.search` builds its backend from the hub and `BM25SearchBackend.search` reaches `fetch_all(self._hub, ...)` (`src/gobby/search/keyword.py:193`) which opens its own pooled sync connection, and `MemoryCrudMixin.get_memories` likewise owns its DB call — neither can execute on a caller-owned async connection. Both therefore factor STATEMENT-RENDERING plus row-mapping helpers — pure functions producing the exact `(sql, params)` (including the explicit scope filter of the keyword scope contract) and mapping result rows — consumed unchanged by the existing sync surfaces and by `related.py`'s async execution; `related.py` is the first async consumer, and rendering parity between the two surfaces is asserted directly (1.1.10). On any timeout: cancel the call's task, log one warning naming the page index, elapsed time, and the timed-out channel(s), return the original candidates for that page, and increment the channel's trip counter — after `RELATED_EVIDENCE_CHANNEL_TRIP_LIMIT` consecutive timeouts that channel is skipped for the remainder of the sweep (circuit breaker), so healthy channels keep retrieving while a persistently hung dependency costs at most its own channel's bounded in-flight calls. `RelatedEvidenceSession` (context manager) remains the sweep-scoped holder — per-channel breaker state, the page semaphore, the outstanding-task set, and the live-connection set used for close-time escalation — created by the sweep owner (1.3's `_attach_related_evidence`) once per sweep and closed in `finally`; 2.2's write path creates one per background invocation; `gather_related_evidence(..., session: RelatedEvidenceSession)` requires it. **Awaitable session close (R11 F3, simplified R15 F1)**: `await session.aclose()` cancels the session's outstanding tasks — async cancellation aborts vector I/O at the transport (R13 F2), and DB tasks follow 0.3's cancel-then-hard-close — hard-closes any still-registered connection (a local `pgconn.finish()` that fails the in-flight wait immediately), and awaits the task set under `RELATED_EVIDENCE_DRAIN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 7.0`, a bound the local-close contract meets unconditionally; nothing — task, connection, or thread — survives `aclose`. The daemon's default executor is never touched. Top-level try/except returns originals with one `logger.warning`.
- **No temporal decay, no content truncation of evidence.**
- **Scope isolation (E1/F1)**: the typed `scope` is applied identically in keyword, vector, and hydration. Project-only sweeps yield only that project's rows; project-plus-global additionally yields global rows; global-only yields only global rows. Cross-project evidence would hand the planner authoritative-looking contradictions from the wrong scope. (The NULL-as-global schema redesign itself is deferred to #18490.)

New `VectorStore` method — implemented in `src/gobby/memory/vectorstore_queries.py` (the P0-committed query module, R6 F1), exposed as a short facade delegator so `vectorstore.py` stays under its line budget:

```python
async def search_by_stored_vectors(
    self, ids: list[str], *, limit: int,
    query_filter: Filter | None = None, collection_name: str | None = None,
    timeout: float | None = None,  # absolute remaining-time budget (R8 F1, R13 F2)
) -> dict[str, list[tuple[str, float]]]:
    """Batch-search using vectors already stored for ids; no embedding calls.
    One client.retrieve(ids, with_vectors=True) + one query_batch_points
    (chunked at 50). Missing ids are absent from the result."""
```

**Remote execution is async-native (R13 F2)**: httpx gives the sync client only per-phase limits — the integer construction default configures connect/read/write/pool at 5s EACH, and the read phase is per-read inactivity, so a slow or trickling response can run arbitrarily long without tripping any phase — meaning NO sync-worker contract can put a provable absolute wall-clock bound on a remote call, and a worker past its budget is unabandonable non-daemon state. The remote path therefore uses no threads at all: `vectorstore_client.py` lazily constructs `AsyncQdrantClient` (same URL/settings and the same construction default), and EVERY remote operation across the four vectorstore modules becomes a native await on it — the public `async` API is unchanged for all callers; only the internal `asyncio.to_thread(sync-client…)` offloads are replaced — plus the removal of the single public sync remote surface, `count_sync` (R45 F1, below) — and the direct vectorstore suites move their client mocks to the async shapes. The maintenance module is explicitly in this conversion's scope (R30 F2): `src/gobby/memory/vectorstore_maintenance.py` — the P0-committed home of today's `_prepare_collection_for_rebuild` (`src/gobby/memory/vectorstore.py:552`), `_activate_rebuild_collection` (`:618`), and `_delete_stale_ids`'s scroll/delete pagination (`:905`), each currently an `asyncio.to_thread` sync-client offload — converts every rebuild, alias, scroll, and delete remote call to a native await under the same absolute-deadline and local-mode rules as the query path, and the direct suites' synchronous client mocks (`tests/memory/test_vectorstore.py:157` and `:187` patch the sync `QdrantClient` today) move to async client shapes and gate the conversion. The absolute bound is `asyncio.timeout` on the caller's remaining monotonic budget: cancellation aborts the in-flight request at the transport, which per-phase timeouts cannot; `timeout` is still forwarded to every Qdrant client operation as a server-side hint; `_raise_if_recoverable` handling matches `search()`. `VectorStore.close` awaits the async client's close AFTER background consumers are cancelled (2.2's teardown ordering). Local (path) mode keeps the sync `QdrantClient` via `asyncio.to_thread` for foreground operations only — background vector surfaces are disabled there (below). **Qdrant deadline reality (R11 F2)**: the installed client's per-operation deadline contract is `timeout: int | None` (verified on `retrieve` and `query_batch_points`), so the float remaining-time budget is converted at each Qdrant call boundary as `max(1, ceil(remaining))`; and `collection_exists` accepts NO per-call timeout at all — the REST path forwards nothing and the gRPC path uses the client-construction default — so the lazy remote client is constructed with a bounded integer default, module constant `QDRANT_CLIENT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 5` in `vectorstore_client.py` — a client-side HINT on both transports, never the absolute bound: per-phase httpx limits cannot cap total elapsed time (R13 F2); the absolute bound is the `asyncio.timeout` cancellation above. **The construction default stays 5 (R12 F4, re-grounded R13 F2)**: 5 equals the installed client's own effective default on both transports (REST inherits httpx's `Timeout(5.0)` when no timeout is passed; gRPC uses `DEFAULT_GRPC_TIMEOUT = 5`) — behavior-preserving for every remote operation — and matches `RELATED_EVIDENCE_CALL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`; but it is per-phase/inactivity semantics, so it never carries the drain-soundness argument: absolute bounds come from task cancellation, not from the client default. **Local mode disables stored-vector evidence (R11 F2, revised R12 F5)**: `QdrantClient(path=...)` (the `vectorstore.py:172` construction branch) routes to `QdrantLocal`, which ignores operation timeout kwargs entirely — in-process work with no cooperative cancellation — and executor workers are non-daemon threads Python joins at interpreter exit, so R11's daemon-lifetime worker holding a wedged local operation would make the vector store's close unboundable (`shutdown(wait=True)` hangs; `shutdown(wait=False)` leaves a thread that blocks process exit). Local (path-based) mode therefore has NO stored-vector evidence channel at all: the vector store exposes its construction mode, the session skips creating a vector channel, evidence degrades to keyword+hydration (the keyword channel owns the motivating f0652bc1/ee3b9e52 pair) with a once-per-process warning, and no evidence executor exists for a local operation to strand. Remote mode's vector channel is the executor-free async path above (R13 F2); background dedup follows the same local-mode rule (2.2, R13 F1). **Lazy-initialization escape closed (R8 F1, R13 F2)**: the FIRST stored-vector search of a sweep against an uninitialized client must not do its initialization outside the session's breaker and deadline. Remote lazy initialization (async client construction + `collection_exists`) runs as cancellable awaits under the SAME `asyncio.timeout` remaining budget as the operation that triggered it — one monotonic deadline propagated across initialization and every operation of the call. **Background dedup needs no execution venue of its own (R12 F3, superseded R13 F1/F2)**: with every remote operation async-native, cancelling a background task aborts its Qdrant I/O at the transport — 2.2's shutdown contract consumes exactly this, and no executor threading on `VectorStore.search` exists. **The synchronous count surface is removed (R45 F1)**: `VectorStore.count_sync` (`src/gobby/memory/vectorstore.py:804-819`) is the one remaining public synchronous remote operation — a direct sync `client.count` network call, live through the synchronous stats chain: `memory/services/maintenance.get_stats` (`src/gobby/memory/services/maintenance.py:23-73`) calls it for `vector_count`, the facade passes it through (`src/gobby/memory/facade.py:494-495`), and four public surfaces call the facade — the CLI stats command (`src/gobby/cli/memory/crud.py:207-219`), the MCP `memory_stats` tool (`src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/memory.py:623-631`), the HTTP stats route (`src/gobby/servers/routes/memory.py:316-324`), and the admin status check's `run_db` offload (`src/gobby/servers/routes/admin/_health.py:342`). Retaining it would either hand a coroutine-returning client to a sync call path or keep a second sync remote client alive solely for counting — both violate the all-async remote contract. `count_sync` is deleted, not converted: `get_stats` becomes async and awaits the existing `VectorStore.count()` (`src/gobby/memory/vectorstore.py:793`), preserving the current degradation exactly (`vector_count = -1` on any failure); the facade `get_stats` becomes an async passthrough; the MCP tool and both HTTP surfaces become async defs awaiting it (the admin status check drops its `run_db` offload for a direct await); and the CLI stats command — a synchronous click entry point with no running loop — runs the awaited call in its own owned event loop via `asyncio.run`, the same owned-loop rule as every other sync entry into the async surface. After this conversion no synchronous remote operation remains on any vectorstore module, so the R13 F2 claim holds without exception. **Inherited consumers convert with the chain (R46 F1)**: `MemoryManager` inherits the facade's `get_stats` (class declaration line 63 of the memory manager module — the module itself is untouched), so `tests/storage/test_memory_manager.py` — whose `test_get_stats` calls it synchronously and subscripts the result — awaits it instead, and the admin status endpoint test (`tests/servers/test_http_endpoints.py`) replaces its plain-`MagicMock` stats stub, which a direct `await` would reject, with an awaitable mock; after the conversion the `get_stats` consumer sweep is repeated to prove no synchronous caller remains anywhere.

**Acceptance:**

- 1.1.1 - `gather_related_evidence` pairs the f0652bc1/ee3b9e52 regression shape (low cosine, shared rare lexemes) via the keyword channel, filters older-than-candidate hits, excludes self, caps at top_k, and never raises. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_regression_sandwich_pair_attaches_via_keyword`.
- 1.1.2 - `_distinctive_terms` prioritizes task-ref digits and identifier tokens, drops stopwords, caps at 24 terms deterministically. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_distinctive_terms_priorities`.
- 1.1.3 - `search_by_stored_vectors` issues one retrieve + chunked batch queries, omits missing ids, and degrades on recoverable errors. test: `tests/memory/test_manager_vectorstore.py::test_search_by_stored_vectors`.
- 1.1.4 - Qdrant unavailability degrades to keyword-only evidence; total retrieval failure returns original candidates unchanged. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_vector_unavailable_degrades`.
- 1.1.5 - Parametrized scope-isolation matrix: project-only, project-plus-global, and global-only sweeps each exclude unrelated projects, with wrong-project hits injected independently through keyword, vector, and hydration rejected in every matrix row; global rows appear only in the two cases that request them. Vector payload representations (R7 F2): global rows stored with a null `project_id` payload, an empty-string payload, and a missing payload key each match under global_only and project_and_global and never under project_only; wrong-project payloads never match in any matrix row. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_scope_isolation_matrix`.
- 1.1.6 - Vector relevance floor at the stated `VECTOR_EVIDENCE_MIN_SCORE = 0.35`: fixtures use fixed absolute stored-vector scores — a 0.30 neighbor is rejected, a 0.40 vector-only neighbor is accepted — and the 0.169 regression pair yields zero vector-channel evidence while still attaching via the keyword channel; scores survive to the admission check. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_vector_floor_boundaries`.
- 1.1.7 - `temporal_direction` filters correctly in both directions ("newer" and "older") against the same fixture corpus, and an explicit `anchor_at` replaces `candidate.created_at` as the comparison reference in both directions (R6 F5). test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_temporal_direction`.
- 1.1.8 - No-stall proof: with a deliberately blocking keyword channel, a blocking vector channel, and a blocking hydration call (each exercised separately), the page returns its original candidates within the page deadline, one warning names the timed-out channel, the event loop's default executor stays usable during the block (a sentinel `asyncio.to_thread` task completes promptly), and a subsequent page with healthy channels retrieves evidence. With a blocker that stays unreleased across multiple pages: the circuit breaker trips after `RELATED_EVIDENCE_CHANNEL_TRIP_LIMIT` consecutive timeouts, later pages skip the hung channel while healthy channels still attach evidence (per-channel breaker isolation, R5 F2); NO thread exists at any point (thread accounting across the whole test), concurrently open evidence connections never exceed the page semaphore, and session close (`aclose`) completes within the bounded drain budget — each blocked DB call is cancelled and its dedicated connection hard-closed locally, with zero DB/stub activity after close (R11 F3, R15 F1). DB-channel bounds (R15 F1): a keyword/hydration statement blocked server-side is aborted at the per-call `asyncio.timeout` by 0.3's terminal sequence while the installed `statement_timeout` independently reaps the orphaned server-side query (server hygiene), and the async path executes exactly the rendered `(sql, params)` the sync surface produces (rendering parity, R5 F2). Blocked-first-SET (R14 F1, closed by R15 F1): for EACH DB channel separately, a call blocked on its FIRST `SET LOCAL` round trip — before any timeout is installed — is aborted at the call budget by 0.3's terminal sequence; and with a server whose cancel endpoint is unreachable (the server-side cancel request itself fails on psycopg's separate cancel connection), repeated such calls across MULTIPLE consecutive sessions still terminate at their ORIGINAL budgets — never extended by psycopg's internal cancel timeout (R16 F1) — and close within the drain bound, with stable process-wide thread and fd counts across the repetitions — no abandonment accumulation of any kind (R15 F1). Hub-pool independence (replaces R6 F3): with the hub's sync pool fully occupied by foreign holders, evidence calls proceed unaffected on their dedicated connections and never acquire from the pool. Session ownership (R7 F1): `gather_related_evidence` requires the caller-supplied `session` keyword argument and never constructs or closes a `RelatedEvidenceSession` internally — consecutive pages passed the same session observe shared breaker state (a trip accumulated on earlier pages suppresses the channel on later pages of the SAME session, while a fresh session starts with untripped breakers), proving the sweep-scoped object is actually reused. Uninitialized-client escape (R8 F1): with a VectorStore whose client is NOT yet initialized and a lazy initialization that blocks, the first stored-vector page returns its original candidates within the page deadline, the vector channel's trip counter increments, the initialization runs as a cancellable await under the page's remaining budget — the default executor stays usable during the block (the sentinel `asyncio.to_thread` task still completes promptly) — and no vector thread exists to strand (R13 F2). Qdrant deadline contract (R11 F2, R13 F2): the per-operation timeout passed to the real client call shapes is a positive integer derived from the fractional remaining budget (`max(1, ceil(remaining))`), the lazily constructed remote async client carries the construction default (`QDRANT_CLIENT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 5`), and the absolute bound is proven against a REAL local REST stub that accepts the connection and trickles bytes indefinitely — every httpx phase stays under its per-phase limit while total elapsed exceeds the call budget — the stored-vector call, and separately a lazy initialization stuck in `collection_exists` against the same stub, is aborted at the `asyncio.timeout` deadline by cancellation, the vector channel's trip counter increments, `aclose` and store close complete with no lingering vector task, and the stub records zero request activity after close (no post-teardown client access). Local-mode degradation (R12 F5): a path-mode vector store yields NO vector channel — zero vector-channel workers are ever created across two sweeps (thread accounting), keyword and hydration evidence still attach, and the degradation warning logs exactly once per process. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_qdrant_timeout_integer_contract`. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_trickling_response_aborted_at_absolute_deadline`. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_local_mode_keyword_only_degradation`. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_uninitialized_client_blocking_init`. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_session_caller_owned`. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_blocking_channel_deadline`. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_persistent_blocker_bounded_connections`. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_statement_timeout_server_hygiene`. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_blocked_first_set_local_bounded_release`. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_cancel_failure_no_accumulation`. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_hub_pool_independence`.
- 1.1.9 - Keyword scope SQL contract: `global_only` queries only NULL-project rows, `project_only` only the named project, and `project_and_global` exactly those two buckets — the rendered predicate is EXECUTED against the real PostgreSQL `memories` schema (nullable UUID `project_id`), proving no UUID-to-empty-string comparison is generated (R5 F1); starvation regression: with more than `related_evidence_fetch_limit` higher-ranked wrong-project keyword matches present, a valid global match still attaches under `global_only` and `project_and_global`. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_keyword_scope_sql_contract`. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_keyword_global_not_starved`.
- 1.1.10 - Rendering parity (R5 F2, revised R15 F1): the statement-rendering helpers produce byte-identical `(sql, params)` for the sync surfaces and `related.py`'s async execution across every scope variant (keyword search and `get_memories` hydration), the sync surfaces' behavior is unchanged (their existing suites pass), and `related.py` opens its connections exclusively from the hub's `conninfo` (0.3) — never the sync pool. test: `tests/memory/test_dream_related.py::test_rendered_statement_parity`. test: `tests/search/test_unified.py`.
- 1.1.11 - Maintenance-module conversion gate (R30 F2): no `asyncio.to_thread` sync-client offload remains in `src/gobby/memory/vectorstore_maintenance.py`'s remote path — every rebuild, alias, scroll, and delete remote operation executes as a native await on the async client under the same absolute-deadline and local-mode rules as the query path — and the direct vectorstore suites pass with async client mocks in place of the synchronous `QdrantClient` patches. test: `tests/memory/test_vectorstore.py`. test: `tests/memory/test_vectorstore_init.py`.
- 1.1.12 - Sync count surface removed (R45 F1): `count_sync` no longer exists on any vectorstore module and `get_stats` awaits the async `VectorStore.count()` with the `-1` degradation preserved on failure; all four public stats surfaces return `vector_count` through the async chain — the CLI stats command via its owned loop, the MCP `memory_stats` tool, the HTTP stats route, and the admin status check awaiting directly without the `run_db` offload. test: `tests/memory/test_maintenance_update.py`. test: `tests/memory/test_vectorstore.py`. test: `tests/cli/test_memory_cli.py`. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/test_memory_tools.py`. test: `tests/servers/routes/test_memory_routes.py`. test: `tests/servers/routes/test_admin.py`. Existing consumers convert with the chain (R46 F1): the storage-manager test awaits `get_stats` through the inheriting `MemoryManager`, and the admin endpoint test stubs stats with an awaitable mock. test: `tests/storage/test_memory_manager.py`. test: `tests/servers/test_http_endpoints.py`.

### 1.2 Remove dream content truncation and add batch-split guard [category: code]
`kind: deliverable`

Target: `src/gobby/memory/dream/models.py`, `src/gobby/memory/dream/planner.py`, `tests/memory/test_dream.py`

- Add frozen dataclass `RelatedMemoryEvidence` (id, memory_type, created_at, newer_by_days, **full content — never truncated**, matched_via) with `to_prompt_dict()`; add `DreamCandidate.related: tuple[RelatedMemoryEvidence, ...] = ()`; `DreamCandidate.to_prompt_dict()` emits `related_newer_memories` only when non-empty.
- **Delete `CONTENT_TRUNCATE_LIMIT` (=1600), `CONTENT_TRUNCATION_MARKER`, and `_truncate_content`** (`src/gobby/memory/dream/models.py:12,13,112`); `to_prompt_dict` renders full candidate content. 74 active memories exceed 1600 chars today (max 3589) and `refresh` rewrites from the rendered view — partial rendering is a live data-loss path.
- Batch-split guard in `src/gobby/memory/dream/planner.py`: before dispatching a batch to `_call_llm_planner`, if the rendered candidates JSON exceeds `planner_batch_max_chars` (config, default 100_000), split the batch in half recursively. **Irreducible terminal case (F3)**: when `len(page) == 1`, dispatch the intact candidate once even when it exceeds the soft limit, log an oversize warning with the candidate id and rendered size, and never recurse further — never skip, never truncate. Content stays whole; only batch shape changes.

**Acceptance:**

- 1.2.1 - Candidate and evidence content render in full with no truncation marker; the old 1600-char truncation test is replaced by a full-content assertion. test: `tests/memory/test_dream.py::test_prompt_renders_full_content`.
- 1.2.2 - Oversized batches split recursively without altering any item's content, down to single-candidate batches. test: `tests/memory/test_dream.py::test_batch_split_guard`.
- 1.2.3 - A single candidate whose candidate-plus-evidence JSON exceeds `planner_batch_max_chars` produces exactly one planner call with byte-for-byte complete content plus an oversize warning. test: `tests/memory/test_dream.py::test_single_item_oversize_dispatches_intact`.
- 1.2.4 - `to_prompt_dict` omits `related_newer_memories` when empty and emits JSON-safe ISO datetimes when present. test: `tests/memory/test_dream.py::test_prompt_dict_related_evidence`.

### 1.3 Wire evidence into both sweep paths with config knobs [category: code] (depends: 1.1)
`kind: deliverable`

Target: `src/gobby/memory/dream/service.py`, `src/gobby/memory/dream/candidates.py`, `src/gobby/storage/memories_dreams.py`, `src/gobby/cli/memory/dream.py`, `src/gobby/config/persistence.py`, `tests/config/test_persistence.py`, `tests/memory/test_dream.py`, `tests/storage/test_memories_dreams.py` (new)

- New `MemoryDreamService._attach_related_evidence(candidates, options, include_global)`: returns candidates unchanged when `related_evidence_enabled` is False, else delegates to `gather_related_evidence`. The sweep owner creates ONE sweep-scoped `RelatedEvidenceSession` (1.1's connection + circuit-breaker holder, R15 F1) per sweep and closes it in `finally` (R4 F2); every page of that sweep shares it — the dry run's paginated loop below is one sweep for this purpose. Call attachment immediately after `list_sweep_candidates(...)` in **both** `_stream_sweep` (~line 698) and `_execute_dry_run` (~line 751) — the dry-run path is where nightly plan-only verdicts surface.
- **Dry-run full-coverage pagination (R9 F1, R11 F1)**: today `_execute_dry_run` reads exactly ONE page (`summary["pages"] = 1 if candidates else 0`) and persists only `action_sample[:50]` (`MAX_ACTION_SAMPLE = 50`), and `gobby memory dream status` (`src/gobby/cli/memory/dream.py`) prints only the summary — never the plan payload — so V3's gate ("Josh reviews the complete proposed action set") is unsatisfiable for any corpus larger than one page. Live pagination advances only because stamping removes rows from the selection window; a dry run never stamps, so it needs a non-mutating traversal. **A keyset cursor over selection columns is not snapshot-safe (R11 F1)**: R9's `after=(last_dreamed_at, updated_at, id)` cursor pages on mutable values — this plan's own `mark_memories_due` clears `last_dreamed_at` and any ordinary edit bumps `updated_at` — so between pages a due row could move ahead of the cursor (omitted) or behind it (reviewed twice), and each page runs as its own query with no shared transaction snapshot. The dry run instead materializes an immutable ID snapshot: `list_dream_candidates` (`src/gobby/storage/memories_dreams.py`) gains `id ASC` as the final ORDER BY key (strict total order), and a new ids-only variant `list_dream_candidate_ids` runs ONCE at dry-run start returning the complete ordered eligible id list; `_execute_dry_run` pages by slicing that list, hydrating each chunk by id via `list_sweep_candidates` (`src/gobby/memory/dream/candidates.py`, which gains the hydrate-by-ids form preserving snapshot order — rows hard-deleted or hidden after the snapshot hydrate as missing and are skipped with a log line, no longer reviewable). Rows mutated mid-run are reviewed once with their current content; rows becoming eligible mid-run wait for the next sweep — every start-of-run eligible id is reviewed exactly once. The loop runs under the single sweep-scoped session, accumulates EVERY page's validated actions, and persists the complete validated action list in the run's plan payload (dry-run only — the live path's 50-item informational sample is unchanged); `gobby memory dream status` renders the complete dry-run action list from the plan payload. `_stream_sweep` is untouched (stamp-driven draining).
- **Shared-file ownership (R9 F1)**: `src/gobby/storage/memories_dreams.py` is also written by 2.1 (connection-accepting `mark_dreamed` variant) and 2.2 (`mark_memories_due`), and `src/gobby/memory/dream/candidates.py` also by 2.2 (`dream_due_version` threading); the 1.3 → 2.1 → 2.2 dependency edges totally order the writers, with 2.2 the final owner of both files and of the storage dream-helpers test module.
- `MemoryDreamConfig` (`src/gobby/config/persistence.py:264`) new fields with inline bounds (style: `MemoryKnowledgeGraphConfig.max_rebuild_concurrency`): `related_evidence_enabled: bool = True`, `related_evidence_top_k: int = 3` (ge=1, le=10), `related_evidence_fetch_limit: int = 10` (ge=1, le=50), `planner_batch_max_chars: int = 100_000` (ge=10_000), and `write_supersession_mark_due_enabled: bool = True` (consumed by 2.2 — ALL new dream config fields land here so 2.2 never touches `persistence.py`; F6). Pydantic defaults are the install-time pre-load: no install YAML template carries dream config keys (verified — `redream_after_hours` appears only in `persistence.py`), so no template change is needed.

**Acceptance:**

- 1.3.1 - Both `_stream_sweep` and `_execute_dry_run` invoke evidence attachment once per page under a single sweep-scoped `RelatedEvidenceSession` (created once per sweep, closed in `finally`) and skip it when disabled; the SAME session object is passed to every page's `gather_related_evidence` call (asserted by identity across a multi-page sweep) and only the sweep owner closes it (R7 F1). test: `tests/memory/test_dream.py::test_sweeps_attach_related_evidence`.
- 1.3.2 - New config fields exist with defaults and bounds validation. test: `tests/config/test_persistence.py::TestMemoryDreamConfig`.
- 1.3.3 - Dry-run full-coverage regression (R9 F1, R11 F1): with more than `page_size` due candidates and more than 50 validated actions, the dry run reviews EVERY start-of-run eligible candidate exactly once (an action for a later-page candidate is present), the persisted plan payload carries the complete validated action list (an action beyond index 50 is retrievable), `gobby memory dream status` output contains that later action, and no candidate's `last_dreamed_at` changes (zero mutations); the ids-only snapshot query's strict total order is proven with the `id` tiebreaker on rows sharing identical `(last_dreamed_at, updated_at)` pairs. Snapshot interleaving (R11 F1): between pages, a concurrent `mark_memories_due` clears `last_dreamed_at` on a not-yet-reviewed snapshot row and a concurrent update bumps another snapshot row's `updated_at` — every start-of-run eligible id still appears exactly once in the reviewed action set (no omission, no double review), and a row hard-deleted between pages is skipped with a log line rather than failing the sweep. test: `tests/memory/test_dream.py::test_dry_run_full_coverage_pagination`. test: `tests/memory/test_dream.py::test_dry_run_snapshot_interleaving`. test: `tests/storage/test_memories_dreams.py::test_list_dream_candidate_ids_snapshot_order`.

### 1.4 Amend dream prompt: evidence signal and access_count correction [category: config] (depends: 1.5)
`kind: deliverable`

Target: `src/gobby/install/shared/prompts/memory/dream.md`, `tests/memory/test_dream.py`

Three amendments (front-matter unchanged):
1. New section "Related newer memories" after "Current truth": candidates may carry `related_newer_memories` — newer memories from the same scope sharing distinctive terms or meaning. A newer memory (especially `memory_type: decision`) that contradicts or supersedes the candidate is a concrete, citable obsolescence signal: cite its id in `reason` and choose `delete` (no residual value) or `refresh` (rewrite to state current truth, referencing the newer memory). Absent/empty `related_newer_memories` is NOT evidence of currentness.
2. Extend the delete-signal list in "Decision procedure": "a newer related memory records a decision or state change that contradicts or supersedes this memory".
3. New numbered rule: "High `access_count` is never evidence of correctness: recall frequency measures retrieval, not truth, and a wrong-but-popular memory self-reinforces. Only `access_count` at or near zero may corroborate a `delete`; never use high `access_count` to justify `keep` against a concrete obsolescence signal." Soften the existing "high age_days with access_count at or near zero" wording to corroborating-not-required when a contradiction signal exists.

**Acceptance:**

- 1.4.1 - Rendered prompt contains the related-evidence obsolescence-signal section and the access_count rule. test: `tests/memory/test_dream.py::test_prompt_contract_evidence_and_access_count`.

## P2: Write-Time Supersession
`kind: framing`

**Goal**: New knowledge reconciles the memories it invalidates at write time instead of waiting for a nightly sweep to notice. Explicit supersession (2.1) consumes none of P1's retrieval machinery, but it shares the memory-CRUD storage module with 1.1 and the dream-stamp storage module with 1.3, so 2.1 depends on 1.1 and 1.3 and is never executable in parallel with either (R8 F2, R9 F1); auto mark-due (2.2) additionally consumes P1's retrieval module.

Thread `supersedes: list[str] | None = None` through the COMPLETE create chain (F5): MCP tool `create_memory` (`src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/memory.py:175`) → facade `MemoryManagerFacadeMethods.create_memory` (`src/gobby/memory/facade.py:136`) → `MemoryLifecycleService.create_memory` (`src/gobby/memory/services/lifecycle.py`) → `MemoryBackendProtocol` (`src/gobby/memory/protocol.py`) / `StorageAdapter.create` (`src/gobby/memory/backends/storage_adapter.py`, plus the null backend) → storage `MemoryCrudMixin.create_memory` (`src/gobby/storage/memories_crud.py:28`), and HTTP route (`src/gobby/servers/routes/memory.py:272`). Document the parameter in the MCP tool schema description so agents recording removals/decisions use it.

**Bounded cardinality (R13 F7)**: module constant `MAX_SUPERSEDES_IDS = 20` in `src/gobby/storage/memories_crud.py`. Every public boundary — the MCP tool and the HTTP route — deduplicates the ids (duplicates count once), validates UUID shape, and rejects more than the cap of unique ids BEFORE any transaction, advisory lock, row lock, or mutation; storage revalidates the same bound as defense in depth. One call therefore acquires at most cap+1 advisory keys, locks at most cap+1 rows, and generates post-commit secondary purge work bounded by the same cap — an erroneous or adversarial list can no longer monopolize a transaction or fan out unbounded cleanup.

**Committed change-listener boundary (R13 F8, matching 2.2's R10 F4 contract)**: every storage path this transaction absorbs notifies memory change listeners today after its self-owned transaction (`mark_dreamed` → `_notify_listeners` at `memories_dreams.py:47`; the CRUD create/update paths likewise), so the connection-accepting variants used inside the supersession transaction NEVER notify in-transaction — a pre-commit notification would announce state that can still roll back. `MemoryStoreBase` (`src/gobby/storage/memories_base.py`) gains the one-line public `notify_changed()` wrapper HERE (2.2's atomic apply primitive consumes the same wrapper, ordered by its `(depends: 2.1)` edge). The outer supersession operation emits exactly ONE `notify_changed()` after a successful commit whenever ANY row or tag changed — created, reactivated, and deduped results with real soft-hides or tag merges each notify once — and emits NONE for a full provenance-satisfied no-op retry or for any rollback.

**MCP guard interaction (R8 F4)**: the MCP `create_memory` tool currently returns success BEFORE the lifecycle runs for two guarded shapes — `is_ephemeral_implementation_note` content returns `{"success": true, "skipped": true}` and `_speculative_memory_task_title` content is redirected to a task — so a `supersedes` list attached to guarded content would be silently ignored while reporting success. A non-empty `supersedes` is explicit persistence intent: it bypasses BOTH guards and invokes the complete create chain (a writer deliberately recording a decision or removal is neither ephemeral nor speculative); empty/None `supersedes` preserves today's guard behavior exactly.

**Secondary-index reconciliation (R8 F5, R10 F2, revised R13 F5)**: the atomic transaction soft-hides superseded rows in PostgreSQL only. Vector and graph artifacts are purged post-commit via `MemoryLifecycleService.purge_secondary_indices` (best-effort, idempotent, faults logged never raised) — without that, known-obsolete content burns retrieval slots for the entire purge-grace window, the exact pathology this plan exists to stop. **Crossrefs take the retain-and-filter form instead (R13 F5, revising R10 F2)**: `memory_crossrefs` rows are RETAINED until hard purge (the existing `ON DELETE CASCADE`), and the starvation defect is fixed at its root — `get_crossrefs` (`src/gobby/storage/memories_crossrefs.py`) joins `memories` on BOTH counterpart rows and filters `deleted_at IS NULL` BEFORE `ORDER BY similarity DESC LIMIT`, so hidden rows of ANY kind stop consuming the result window (`CrossrefService.get_related`'s post-hydration rejection stays as defense in depth) and a restored row's crossrefs are live again with zero rebuild; the R10 F2 crossref-delete step is dropped. **Purge and restore serialize on the row (R13 F5)**: superseded rows are recoverable soft-hides, yet today's public restore chain (MCP/HTTP → facade → storage `restore_memory`, `src/gobby/storage/memories_dreams.py:87`) only reactivates the PostgreSQL row — a restored superseded memory would be active while invisible to vector and graph recall. Each per-id purge step therefore runs a short transaction taking `SELECT deleted_at FROM memories WHERE id = %s FOR UPDATE`, proceeds only when the row EXISTS and is still hidden, performs the vector and graph deletes while holding that lock, and commits; the storage restore reactivates under the same `FOR UPDATE`. **A missing row is SKIPPED by the supersession purge, never cleaned (R14 F3, scoped R21 F4)**: a missing row acquires no lock, and memory ids are deterministic uuid5(content, scope), so a concurrent same-content create can legitimately recreate the id and upsert fresh vector/graph artifacts between an unfenced missing-row check and a late delete — skipping closes the recreate race for the supersession purge with zero new mechanism. Physically deleted rows' secondary cleanup belongs to the hard-delete owners, and their surface CANNOT reuse this skip (R21 F4): ordinary delete physically removes the row BEFORE its secondary cleanup ever runs, and the dream hard purge does the same — a missing-row skip there would skip every legitimate hard-delete cleanup — so the hard-delete owners use the distinct recreate-safe cleanup contract defined below. **The whole per-id cleanup runs under one absolute budget on the async DB surface (R14 F4, R15 F2)**: 1.1's async-client deadline bounds only Qdrant — the graph path (the Falkor client is `falkordb.asyncio`, already native awaits) has only a per-I/O-wait `socket_timeout` and `remove_memory_from_graph` issues multiple queries (scope lookup, DETACH DELETE, orphan cleanup) — and `asyncio.timeout` cannot preempt a synchronous psycopg row-lock acquisition on the event-loop thread, while offloading it to a thread would recreate the unterminatable-worker class R15 F1 eliminated. Each per-id purge step therefore runs its visibility transaction through 0.3's `run_bounded_db` with `lock_timeout` requested: a dedicated async connection, `SET LOCAL lock_timeout` and `statement_timeout` installed at the remaining budget, then the `SELECT deleted_at ... FOR UPDATE`, the Qdrant delete, and every Falkor query all under ONE `asyncio.timeout` monotonic budget, module constant `SUPERSESSION_CLEANUP_BUDGET_SECONDS = 5.0` in `src/gobby/memory/services/lifecycle.py`. On expiry, cancellation aborts the in-flight await at the transport and the connection is hard-closed with the follow-up second cancellation per 0.3's terminal sequence (R16 F1) — the server aborts the uncommitted transaction on EOF, releasing the row lock (a still-waiting acquisition is additionally reaped by `lock_timeout`), the event loop stays responsive throughout (no sync DB call ever runs on it), no worker or connection survives, and the fault is logged — the purge is idempotent and repaired by retry (including after 0.3's typed indeterminate-commit outcome, R16 F2 — re-purge converges whether or not the interrupted commit applied). The public restore chain then REBUILDS what supersession purged — and reconciles content that changed while hidden (R17 F3): a hidden-row content update applies its PRIMARY effect while the fenced reconciliation skips wholesale, and supersession deliberately RETAINS crossrefs, so a superseded row updated from content A to content B while hidden would otherwise expose A's old links and similarity scores as active B crossrefs on restore. The durable content-dirty signal already exists — the primary update sets `vector_needs_reindex = TRUE` (`memories_crud.py:493`) and the skipped reconciliation never clears it. **Restore is itself a first-class fenced reconciliation (R18 F1)**: today's chain — a row-only reactivation in storage, a facade that exposes only the row flip, and a lifecycle rebuild helper that re-embeds and marks the graph pending as a separate UNFENCED best-effort sequence — leaves the restore rebuild both unfenced (a delayed rebuild entering after a newer content update, rescope, or re-supersession would overwrite the newer owner's vector/payload or re-add hidden artifacts) and undurable (a content-clean restore whose rebuild fails leaves an active row with no repair intent, since the flag need not have been set before restore). The reactivating `FOR UPDATE` transaction therefore (i) reads the pre-restore dirty flag, (ii) deletes the RETAINED stale crossrefs when that flag is set (R17 F3), and (iii) stamps `vector_needs_reindex = TRUE` unconditionally as durable full-reconciliation intent BEFORE any external effect, then commits reactivation. The post-commit restore rebuild runs as a third instance of the SAME fenced reconciliation (the R15 F3 fence below, with its R17 F2 staleness validation and R17 F4 convergence): under the held fence it proceeds only when the row is still ACTIVE with content AND scope equal to the restored snapshot's — any mismatch skips wholesale, leaving the newer mutation's own reconciliation or the newer supersession's purge the owner of final secondary state — performs the embed-and-upsert and pending-graph mark, rebuilds crossrefs from the restored content exactly when the reactivating transaction's crossref delete fired, and clears the intent via the content-CAS as its LAST SQL statement committing with the fence; a hidden row restored WITHOUT an intervening content change keeps its retained crossrefs with zero rebuild (R13 F5 preserved). A restore rebuild that fails or is terminated at ANY point leaves the reactivation-committed intent set, and the repair driver (R17 F4) converges the row with NO further user mutation. Both interleavings are safe: purge locking first → the blocked restore completes and its rebuild repairs the deletes; restore committing first → the late purge observes an active row and skips — rebuilt state can never be purged; and a same-content recreate after hard purge is safe the same way — the purge observes a missing row and skips, so the recreated memory's fresh artifacts are never deleted (R14 F3). After a successful superseding storage commit, `MemoryLifecycleService` calls the visibility-locked purge for EVERY deduplicated supersedes id — including provenance-satisfied ids on an idempotent retry, so a crash between commit and cleanup is repaired by re-purge. Secondary-store failures log and never roll back or convert the committed create into a failure.

**Ordinary mutators' secondary effects are visibility-fenced in full (R15 F3, extended R16 F3)**: the row locks fence mutators only through their primary PostgreSQL commit — their secondary effects run AFTER commit, and today none are visibility-aware: `update_memory` fetches with `visibility="all"` and `_refresh_content_indices` unconditionally embed-upserts to Qdrant, marks vectors reindexed, removes the row from the graph (`remove_memory_from_graph`), calls `mark_pending_graph`, deletes crossrefs (`delete_crossrefs`), and optionally rebuilds them (`rebuild_for_memory`); `rescope_memory`'s `sync_memory_scope_indices` unconditionally sets the Qdrant payload and marks pending; and `get_pending_graph_memories` (`src/gobby/storage/memories_graph.py:125`) selects with NO `deleted_at` filter — so a mutator committing after supersession on a still-hidden row (2.1.13's allowed ordering) can re-add the purged row to vector and graph recall, and the content-refresh path's DESTRUCTIVE tail (graph removal, crossref delete) can land arbitrarily late — after a restore has reactivated and rebuilt the row — leaving an active restored memory graphless and crossref-less (R16 F3). Fix at one seam: the ENTIRE post-commit secondary reconciliation of each mutator path — for content refresh, the Qdrant upsert, `mark_vectors_reindexed`, the graph removal, `mark_pending_graph`, the crossref delete, and the optional crossref rebuild; for rescope, the payload write, the graph-node replacement, the pending mark, and the crossref delete-and-rebuild (R20 F1) — runs inside ONE active-row visibility-and-staleness fence on 0.3's async surface: `SELECT deleted_at, content, project_id FROM memories WHERE id = %s FOR SHARE` on the fence's own bounded transaction — `run_bounded_db` with `lock_timeout` requested under module constant `MUTATOR_RECONCILIATION_BUDGET_SECONDS = 5.0` in `src/gobby/memory/services/lifecycle.py` (R17 F4) — HELD ACROSS THE FULL RECONCILIATION, with hidden-or-missing skipping EVERY secondary mutation for that row, and equally skipping wholesale on STALENESS (R17 F2): `FOR SHARE` fences from two ordinary mutators are mutually compatible, and the lifecycle hands each reconciliation a post-commit snapshot (`_refresh_content_indices(old_memory, memory)` embeds `memory.content` unconditionally; `sync_memory_scope_indices(memory_id, project_id)` writes the snapshot scope), so a DELAYED reconciliation for mutation A entering after newer mutation B would overwrite B's Qdrant payload/vector, graph state, and crossrefs with A's stale snapshot while still observing an active row — the fence therefore validates the row's CURRENT state against the mutation that scheduled it — UNIFORMLY across mutator kinds (R17 F2, R18 F1, R19 F2): every fenced reconciliation — content refresh, rescope, and the restore rebuild — carries its scheduling commit's (content, scope) snapshot and proceeds only when the row is ACTIVE with current content AND scope both equal to that snapshot's; `sync_memory_scope_indices` today receives only `memory_id` and `project_id`, so the rescope path gains the rescope-committed content in its snapshot (returned by the primary rescope UPDATE it already runs), and the terminal content-CAS clear below always executes against the SCHEDULING snapshot's content, never content re-read at reconciliation time — a partial reconciliation of one kind can therefore never clear intent stamped by a newer commit of another kind whose vector/graph/crossref work it did not perform — and any mismatch skips wholesale, leaving the newest mutator — whose own primary commit re-stamped the intent flag and scheduled its own reconciliation — the owner of final secondary state; an ordinary mutator's primary row UPDATE likewise conflicts with the held `FOR SHARE`, so no newer primary commit can land mid-reconciliation — no Qdrant write, no graph removal or requeue, no crossref delete or rebuild (expensive computation — the embedding call — stays outside the fence; the SQL mutations execute on the fence's connection inside its ordering boundary, and the Qdrant/Falkor calls run while the fence lock is held). **The crossref rebuild is one of those SQL mutations (R19 F4)**: `CrossrefService.rebuild_for_memory` delegates to `create`, whose per-link writes each run `create_crossref` through the service's self-owned `_run_storage` path today — rebuilt crossrefs would commit on a foreign connection, surviving a fence rollback or landing after a competing hide/rescope. `CrossrefService` therefore gains a connection-aware write path: the fenced reconciliation passes its held connection down and every `create_crossref` INSERT executes on that connection (a connection-accepting variant in `src/gobby/storage/memories_crossrefs.py`, the same pattern as `mark_dreamed`'s), while the embedding call and the Qdrant similarity search stay outside the SQL ordering boundary as before. **Crossref counterpart rows are locked and revalidated before insertion (R45 F2)**: the fence's `FOR SHARE` covers only the SOURCE row — a candidate counterpart discovered by the similarity search stays freely mutable, so counterpart B can commit a rescope and complete its own fenced crossref delete-and-rebuild between A's search and A's insert, and A's `create_crossref` on the held connection would then resurrect the stale old-scope A–B link AFTER B's cleanup: both terminal CAS clears succeed, no repair intent survives, and because `get_crossrefs`' visibility join filters only `deleted_at IS NULL`, the active-but-rescoped counterpart's link consumes the SQL `LIMIT` window indefinitely — the R20 F1 starvation resurrected on the counterpart axis. The rebuild therefore treats discovery output as unverified hints and admits counterparts only under a single globally ordered lock acquisition (R46 F3, R46 F4): the embedding call and the Qdrant similarity search complete BEFORE any fence row lock is taken, and the fence then acquires its ENTIRE `memories` row-lock set — the source plus every surviving candidate id — in ONE statement, `SELECT id, deleted_at, project_id, content, vector_needs_reindex FROM memories WHERE id = ANY(%s) ORDER BY id FOR SHARE` on the held connection; the source row is never pre-locked separately, and the source's scheduling-snapshot revalidation runs against the row this statement returns — the projection carries `content` precisely so the fence's staleness check needs no second read (R47 F1): the rebuild proceeds only when the locked source row is ACTIVE with `content` and `project_id` both equal to the scheduling snapshot's, the same equality the single-row fence enforces, because content mutators write `content` and bump `updated_at` after the snapshot was taken and a delayed rebuild would otherwise insert links computed from a stale source embedding (a failed revalidation still skips wholesale, leaving the newer mutator's own scheduled reconciliation the owner of final crossref state). Single-statement ascending acquisition is deadlock-free on `memories` rows against every disciplined locker: the supersession primary takes its multi-row `FOR UPDATE` set in one ascending statement, single-row mutators hold one lock and request nothing further, and two transactions that each acquire their complete set in one ascending-id statement cannot form a waits-for cycle — the transaction closing the cycle would have to already hold an id higher than one it still waits for, contradicting in-statement ascending order — so a fence `FOR SHARE` and a supersession `FOR UPDATE` strictly serialize instead of deadlocking, in either id order; the fence takes no advisory locks, so the supersession's advisory-then-row phasing cannot interleave into a cycle either. Under the held locks, admission requires each counterpart to be ACTIVE, scope-eligible under the source's scheduling-snapshot scope (the discovery predicate `_crossref_scope_filter` — a project-scoped source admits same-project or global counterparts; a global source admits only global ones — evaluated against the counterpart's CURRENT row), AND free of pending reindex intent: a counterpart whose `vector_needs_reindex` is TRUE has a stored vector unproven for its committed row, so it is skipped and its own fenced reconciliation rebuilds its links. For the admitted survivors the rebuild retrieves the CURRENT stored vectors in one batched Qdrant retrieve and recomputes each similarity against the source's scheduling-snapshot embedding, inserting only links that meet the threshold NOW — discovery scores are ranking hints only, and every persisted link score is computed from a vector proven current for a share-locked, intent-free counterpart row, so a counterpart content update that commits and completes its own delete-and-rebuild between the source's search and its lock acquisition can no longer leave a stale-scored active link consuming `get_crossrefs`' SQL `LIMIT` window. The held share locks serialize the race in both directions: a counterpart's primary rescope, hide, or content-update UPDATE blocks until the fence commits — its own subsequent fenced rebuild then deletes and rebuilds any just-committed links under its new state — or the fence's lock statement waits out the already-committed write and admission re-checks the current row and current vector.
