A response-lost record whose row a LATER ordinary writer overwrote is never replayed (R47 F2): with the COMMIT applied (hidden row reactivated) and an ordinary content update committing between the import COMMIT and the verification read, the orchestrator issues no further primary mutation — the replay it must NOT run would LWW-lose and return `unchanged`, discarding the owed wakeup — and instead emits exactly one notification and one mark-due anchored at the greater of the record's and the observed row's `updated_at`, so the older related memory created before the reactivation is still marked due. test: `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py::test_import_response_lost_postcommit_update_retains_wakeup`. A response-lost record whose row a LATER hard delete removed is never re-inserted (R47 F2): with the row hard-deleted between the import COMMIT and the verification read, the orchestrator observes absence, resolves the record as isolated-indeterminate with no primary replay, and the memories table stays empty of that id — no resurrection. test: `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py::test_import_response_lost_postcommit_hard_delete_no_resurrection`. And the snapshot canonical-winner gate holds (R48 F1): an untrusted verified snapshot containing an older `_deleted: true` tombstone and a strictly newer live record for the same id applies no delete for that id in either physical order — tombstone-then-live and live-then-tombstone both complete the pass with zero DELETE statements bound for that id and the local row intact — while the converse pins application: a tombstone strictly newer than every same-id live record in the snapshot still applies, and a tombstone tying a live record's timestamp exactly still applies (deletion preferred on ties, the `newer_record` rule); the veto is bounded and resumable: a deadline expiring after batch discovery but before the veto scan completes resumes from the persisted batch winner map, its normalized per-id live maxima, and veto-scan cursor with zero re-discovery, the map bounded by `TOMBSTONE_REPLAY_MAX_RECORDS_PER_CYCLE` entries. test: `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py::test_replay_snapshot_older_tombstone_newer_live_survives_both_orders`. test: `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py::test_replay_snapshot_newer_or_tied_tombstone_still_applies`. test: `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py::test_replay_veto_batch_state_resumes_across_deadline_windows`. And the veto scan admits live records exactly as import does (R49 F1): a live record bearing a strictly newer `created_at` and no `updated_at` vetoes its id's older tombstone in both physical orders — normalization inherits `updated_at` from `created_at` before comparison, the same value import canonicalization feeds `newer_record` — so the row survives replay exactly as it would survive import of the same snapshot; a live record carrying a strictly newer valid `updated_at` that import would exclude — empty or non-string `content`, or an ephemeral implementation note — vetoes nothing in either physical order and the tombstone applies its committed delete, again matching the import outcome; and a live record with neither timestamp is stamped `utc_now()` by the shared admission helper exactly as import stamps it — a concrete persisted timestamp compared through the ordinary `newer_record` ordering, no marker special case: against a past-dated tombstone the stamp wins and the row survives replay in both physical orders, exactly as it survives import; against a future-dated tombstone — admitted by the same any-parseable-`deleted_at` rule import applies, `newer_record` preferring any strictly greater timestamp — the tombstone wins and the committed delete applies, exactly as import deletes (R50 F1). test: `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py::test_replay_veto_created_at_only_live_record_survives_both_orders`. test: `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py::test_replay_veto_inadmissible_live_records_do_not_veto`. test: `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py::test_replay_veto_both_missing_stamp_beats_past_tombstone`. test: `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py::test_replay_veto_future_tombstone_beats_both_missing_stamp`. And the tombstone id boundary holds (R48 F2): a sync-file tombstone whose `id` does not parse as a UUID is rejected visibly at import in the parse phase (`MemoryImportError` naming the line and offending id, raised before any transaction begins, no record of the file applied), is omitted by the merge exporter's canonicalization as a named, counted vacuous-tombstone diagnostic — the export publishes and trust-stamps the remaining generation (R51 F1) — and resolves in the replay as a named, counted invalid-tombstone diagnostic that advances the cursor without binding any database statement — id validation precedes durable-field validation, the pass completes with no stuck cursor across arbitrarily many cycles, and the generation stays untrusted; no arm ever binds a non-UUID id into a `memories.id` predicate. test: `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py::test_import_tombstone_invalid_uuid_id_fails_visibly`. test: `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py::test_export_omits_vacuous_invalid_uuid_tombstone_id`. test: `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py::test_replay_invalid_uuid_tombstone_id_visible_skip_no_wedge`. And the combined over-ceiling invalid-id case terminates end to end (R51 F1): an untrusted generation holding a `_deleted: true` line whose id fails UUID validation and whose serialized length exceeds `TOMBSTONE_REPLAY_MAX_FOREIGN_LINE_BYTES`, followed by a later valid tombstone, first fails the cycle visibly with no cursor advance; one explicit export with default `force=false`, against a corpus whose every file record is already DB-resident so no unrelated DB record masks the count drop, omits the vacuous line as the named diagnostic without tripping the shrink guard (R52 F1: the baseline excludes the counted vacuous omission), publishes and trust-stamps the post-replace generation, and the next replay pass advances past the omission and applies the later tombstone's committed delete. test: `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py::test_replay_advances_after_export_omits_overceiling_invalid_id_tombstone`. And the guard's protection survives the accounting change: an export whose canonical count drops below the omission-adjusted baseline for any non-vacuous reason still raises `MemoryExportError` without `force`. test: `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py::test_export_shrink_guard_still_trips_on_nonvacuous_loss`.
- 2.1.26 - Crossref counterpart lock-and-revalidate (R45 F2; revised R46 F3/F4): with reconciliation A paused after its similarity search returned counterpart B, B's rescope commits and B's fenced reconciliation completes its crossref delete-and-rebuild; resuming A inserts NO A–B link — admission observes B's new scope under the single ascending lock statement and skips it — and B's `get_related` window serves only current-scope counterparts with no stale-link LIMIT starvation; the blocked ordering (A's single lock statement already holds B's row) stalls B's rescope until A commits, after which B's rebuild removes the just-committed link, converging to the same linkless end state; a counterpart hidden between search and lock acquisition is likewise skipped. Same-scope content-update orderings (R46 F3), both ways: with A paused after its search, B's content update commits and B's own rebuild completes before A acquires its locks — A recomputes B's similarity from B's CURRENT stored vector and inserts a link only when it qualifies NOW, and when it does not qualify, B's `get_related` window is proven free of any stale-scored A–B link (LIMIT-starvation assertion); in the blocked ordering B's content update waits on A's held share lock and B's subsequent rebuild owns the final link state. A counterpart with pending `vector_needs_reindex` intent is skipped without insertion (intent-pending arm). Deadlock regression (R46 F4) on real PostgreSQL: a supersession whose target id sorts BELOW a concurrently reconciling crossref source — advisory-then-row `FOR UPDATE` against the fence's single ascending `FOR SHARE` statement — completes in both interleavings with neither operation lost to deadlock or lock-timeout. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_crossref_counterpart_rescope_race`. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_crossref_counterpart_hidden_skip`. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_crossref_counterpart_content_update_race`. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_crossref_counterpart_reindex_intent_skip`. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_crossref_fence_supersession_no_deadlock`. A SOURCE content update interleaving the fence on either side inserts no lasting stale-source link (R47 F1): committing BEFORE lock acquisition, the fence's locked source row fails content revalidation and the rebuild skips wholesale; blocked BEHIND the held `FOR SHARE` until the fence commits, the update's own scheduled reconciliation deletes and rebuilds the links the fence wrote from its then-current snapshot — in both orderings no link computed from the superseded source embedding survives. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_crossref_fence_source_content_update_race`.

### 2.3 Human-friendly CLI supersession (deferred)
`kind: deferred`

The human CLI write path (`gobby memory create`, `src/gobby/cli/memory/crud.py`) gets no supersession surface in this epic — humans don't work with UUIDs, so the UX (interactive related-memory picker + git-style id-prefix resolution) is designed and built separately.

```yaml
deferral:
  task_ref: "#18456"
  reason: "CLI supersession needs a human-friendly UX (interactive picker + id prefixes), deferred to the post-0.5.0 backlog by Josh during enhancement round 1 (suggestion E5)."
  owner: "backend-developer"
  original_acceptance_items:
    - 2.3.1
```

### 2.2 Auto mark-due of related older memories after create [category: code] (depends: 1.1, 1.3, 1.5, 2.1)
`kind: deliverable`

Target: `src/gobby/memory/services/lifecycle.py`, `src/gobby/memory/manager.py`, `src/gobby/runner_lifecycle_shutdown.py`, `src/gobby/storage/memories_base.py`, `src/gobby/storage/memories_dreams.py`, `src/gobby/storage/memories_models.py`, `src/gobby/memory/protocol.py`, `src/gobby/memory/backends/storage_adapter.py`, `src/gobby/memory/facade.py`, `src/gobby/memory/dream/models.py`, `src/gobby/memory/dream/candidates.py`, `src/gobby/memory/dream/protocols.py`, `src/gobby/memory/dream/apply.py`, `src/gobby/memory/dream/storage.py`, `src/gobby/memory/dream/service.py`, `src/gobby/sync/memories.py`, `src/gobby/storage/postgres_baseline_schema.sql`, `src/gobby/storage/migrations/324_memories_dream_due_version.sql` (new), `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py` (new), `tests/storage/test_memories_dreams.py` (new), `tests/storage/test_migration_contract.py`, `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py`

After a create commits, `MemoryLifecycleService` (the actual post-create background-task owner — F6) schedules a background task AFTER storage plus vector upsert have completed (mirror the existing background-dedup fire-and-forget pattern on the facade) that calls `gather_related_evidence` with the new memory as the sole candidate, `temporal_direction="older"` (the 1.1 API parameter — no ad-hoc inversion), and a short-lived `RelatedEvidenceSession` created for the invocation and closed in `finally` (R4 F2); eligible hits get `last_dreamed_at = NULL` via new storage helper `mark_memories_due(ids, *, expected_project_id)` (per-memory variant of `mark_project_memories_due`, `src/gobby/storage/memories_dreams.py:50`). **Scope is revalidated atomically at the UPDATE, never assumed from retrieval (R13 F6)**: a row can be rescoped or soft-hidden between evidence hydration and the mark, so the UPDATE carries `AND deleted_at IS NULL AND project_id IS NOT DISTINCT FROM %s` with the write path's expected scope (the project UUID, or NULL for a global create) — a row failing revalidation is skipped silently (it is no longer eligible), never mutated cross-scope or while hidden; the dream apply fence protects sweeps AFTER mark-due, and this predicate closes the retrieval-to-update boundary before it. No auto-hide — the next dream reviews them with the new memory attached as evidence, behind confidence gates and snapshots. Gated by `MemoryDreamConfig.write_supersession_mark_due_enabled` (field added in 1.3; this task adds no config fields itself). Background failures log and never affect the create.

**Bounded background fan-out (R9 F2, R10 F3)**: the mirrored fire-and-forget pattern (`fire_background_dedup`, `src/gobby/memory/services/lifecycle.py:111`) tracks each `asyncio.create_task` in `_background_tasks` but bounds nothing — one invocation per create, and each mark-due invocation owns a `RelatedEvidenceSession` with its outstanding async tasks and per-call dedicated DB connections (R15 F1), so a normal burst of N creates would amplify into N concurrent sessions and their DB/Qdrant connections. Module constant `WRITE_MARK_DUE_MAX_CONCURRENCY = 2` (no config knob): the background task body acquires a lifecycle-scoped `asyncio.Semaphore(2)` BEFORE constructing its `RelatedEvidenceSession` and closes the session in `finally`; queued invocations hold only their task object — no session, no connections. **Shutdown cancellation must be built, not assumed (R10 F3)**: today NOTHING cancels `_background_tasks` — the set is created in `MemoryManager.__init__` (`src/gobby/memory/manager.py:98`) and only add/discard-on-completion touches it, `MemoryManager.close` (:309) closes just the FalkorDB client, and the daemon shutdown helper (`_close_managers_and_storage` in `src/gobby/runner_lifecycle_shutdown.py` awaits that close under a 5s `wait_for` today) runs BEFORE storage/Qdrant teardown — so queued and active mark-due work would survive manager shutdown and race closing DB/Qdrant resources. This section makes `MemoryManager.close` cancel every task in `_background_tasks`, await the set until drained, and only then close secondary resources; a task cancelled before acquiring the semaphore never constructs a session, and an active task's session close in `finally` runs on cancellation. **Session close is a real drain (R11 F3, simplified R15 F1)**: with the evidence channels async-native there is no synchronous worker anywhere — cancelling a mark-due task aborts its in-flight vector I/O at the transport (R13 F2) and its DB calls via 0.3's cancel-then-hard-close — so cancelling and awaiting `_background_tasks` plus awaiting the sessions' `aclose` genuinely terminates all work before secondary teardown. **The drain is concurrent, not sequential (R12 F2)**: `WRITE_MARK_DUE_MAX_CONCURRENCY` admits TWO active `RelatedEvidenceSession` objects and each `aclose` may consume the full 7s drain budget — awaiting them one after another could need 14s, breaching the 10s runner budget — so close snapshots the active sessions and awaits all their `aclose()` drains concurrently (`asyncio.gather`) under one shared bounded shutdown drain, bounding the whole drain phase at a single budget. **Every task class in the shared registry aborts its I/O (R12 F3, revised R13 F1)**: `_background_tasks` also carries the pre-existing memory-dedup tasks (`fire_background_dedup` adds them at `lifecycle.py:141-142`), and background dedup reaches the vector store's search AND — on its similar-update and fallback-store paths — embed-and-upsert with its lazy initialization, all of which run through `asyncio.to_thread` today, so cancelling the dedup awaiter would leave untracked default-executor Qdrant workers still using the client into secondary teardown. With 1.1's remote-async conversion there is nothing to thread or drain: every remote Qdrant operation background dedup reaches — lazy initialization, search, and upsert — is a cancellable await, so `MemoryManager.close`'s cancel-and-await of `_background_tasks` aborts the I/O itself at the transport (R13 F1/F2). In local (path) mode `QdrantLocal` work is uncancellable in-process execution, so background vector dedup is disabled the same way 1.1 disables local stored-vector evidence: `fire_background_dedup` skips scheduling when the vector store reports path mode, logging a once-per-process warning — no dedup task, no Qdrant worker, no strandable state; the create's own storage write and its foreground indexing are untouched. Only after `_background_tasks` drains to empty and the session drains complete does secondary teardown — including `VectorStore.close`, which closes the async client — begin. The runner budget gains explicit margin: this section raises `_close_managers_and_storage`'s `wait_for` around `memory_manager.close()` from 5s to 10s (> the 7s drain budget), adding `src/gobby/runner_lifecycle_shutdown.py` to its targets — 3.4 also writes that module (refresh-lifecycle registration), and 3.4's existing `(depends: 2.2)` edge orders the writers with 3.4 the final owner. Active sessions never exceed the cap; concurrently open evidence DB connections never exceed the cap times the session page semaphore (4), and no mark-due thread exists at any point (R15 F1).

**Write-path scope mapping (R2 F4)**: a project-scoped create supplies `RetrievalScope.project_only(project_id)`; a global create supplies `RetrievalScope.global_only()`. A create never widens to cross-scope evidence — the isolation contract of 1.1 applies verbatim.

**Outcome gating (R2 F4, R3 F2)**: consumes 2.1's typed `MemoryWriteResult` exactly; mark-due is scheduled only for `created` or `reactivated` outcomes (new active knowledge). A `deduped` outcome — content deduping to an existing active memory — schedules nothing, so repeated duplicate creates cannot repeatedly clear `last_dreamed_at` on older memories. **Temporal anchor per outcome (R6 F5)**: storage reactivation preserves the row's original `created_at` and stamps `updated_at` with the reactivation time (`memories_crud.py` `ON CONFLICT` reactivation branch), so anchoring "older" on `created_at` would miss every memory created between the original creation and the reactivation. The write path passes 1.1's `anchor_at` explicitly: a `created` outcome anchors on the new row's `created_at`; a `reactivated` outcome anchors on the row's post-reactivation `updated_at`. Dream-review chronology is untouched — sweeps never pass `anchor_at`.

**Imported new knowledge wakes older memories too (R25 F2)**: the JSONL import boundary produces the same typed outcomes as ordinary creates, but its records enter storage directly — no lifecycle create ever fires — and 2.1's import orchestrator runs only the fenced secondary reconciliation, so a fresh or reactivated import would leave older contradicted memories sleeping with `last_dreamed_at` intact, bypassing exactly the write-time wakeup this section builds. Once this section's scheduler exists, the import orchestrator (`src/gobby/sync/memories.py`, written by 2.1 and finalized here per the shared-ownership order) schedules the SAME outcome-aware mark-due hook for each imported `created` or `reactivated` outcome after that record's fenced reconciliation completes: identical scope mapping (the imported row's project or global scope), identical outcome-specific anchors (created → the row's `created_at`; reactivated → its post-reactivation `updated_at`), and the same lifecycle semaphore, session bounds, failure isolation, and shutdown tracking — an import burst cannot exceed `WRITE_MARK_DUE_MAX_CONCURRENCY` any more than a create burst can. `updated`, `unchanged`, and `deduped` imports schedule nothing: ordinary content updates never mark due, and a winning LWW replace keeps ordinary update semantics. On the daemon loop the scheduled invocations are ordinary tracked background work under the existing shutdown drain; on the CLI-owned import loop (R25 F1) the orchestrator awaits its scheduled mark-due invocations before the owned loop closes — R25 F1's no-post-return-activity contract includes them.

**Lost-wakeup guard (R3 F3, R4 F4)**: `mark_memories_due` must not race an in-flight sweep — today `mark_dreamed` stamps `last_dreamed_at` unconditionally, AND the live apply path mutates BEFORE stamping (`_refresh` rewrites content/tags via `update_memory` first, `_promote` rescopes first, soft-hide snapshots first — `src/gobby/memory/dream/apply.py`), so a CAS on `mark_dreamed` alone would still let a stale refresh or promote land while merely leaving the mutated row due. Contract: add `dream_due_version INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` to `memories` — baseline schema plus migration `324_memories_dream_due_version.sql` (R5 F6: the sequence ends at 323 today; 3.4's rename takes 325 and depends on this section, which serializes the shared baseline-schema and migration-contract edits; 3.4 is the final owner of those shared files after 324 lands); `mark_memories_due` increments it while clearing `last_dreamed_at`. The version is threaded end-to-end so apply can see what selection saw: `SELECT` → `Memory.from_row` (`src/gobby/storage/memories_models.py`) → `MemoryRecord` (`src/gobby/memory/protocol.py`) → `StorageAdapter._to_record` (`src/gobby/memory/backends/storage_adapter.py`) → `memory_to_candidate` (`src/gobby/memory/dream/candidates.py`) → `DreamCandidate` (`src/gobby/memory/dream/models.py`) → the dream manager protocol (`src/gobby/memory/dream/protocols.py`) and the apply/facade boundary (`src/gobby/memory/dream/apply.py`, connection-accepting `mark_dreamed` variant surfaced through `src/gobby/memory/facade.py`). **Version fence on every apply mutation — one atomic primitive (R5 F4)**: `MemoryDreamStore` (`src/gobby/memory/dream/storage.py`) gains ONE connection-aware atomic apply primitive that each mutating action — keep cursor-advance, review/delete soft-hide, refresh, promote — runs through: a single transaction that locks the row and verifies the FULL selected state (R6 F6 — `dream_due_version` fences only concurrent mark-due calls; a manual content/tag edit, rescope, or soft-hide after candidate selection changes none of it, so a version-only fence would let an LLM action derived from stale content overwrite a newer manual update): `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE WHERE id = %s AND dream_due_version = %s AND updated_at = %s AND project_id IS NOT DISTINCT FROM %s AND deleted_at IS NULL`, with the selected candidate's `updated_at` and `project_id` (already carried by `DreamCandidate`) threaded alongside the version. On match it reads the before-row, inserts the snapshot, performs the row mutations, stamps via the connection-accepting `mark_dreamed` variant (from 2.1), reads the after-row, and completes the snapshot — all on that connection. The store's snapshot insert/complete/read methods currently open their own transactions and cannot participate in a fence, so they gain connection-accepting variants used by the primitive. Secondary-index effects — the Qdrant/graph/crossref reconciliation that refresh and promote reach today through lifecycle methods — run only AFTER the fenced transaction commits; mismatch and rollback paths emit no secondary effects. On mismatch: nothing runs — no snapshot row, no content/tag/scope change, no stamp — and the row stays due for the next sweep, which re-reviews it with the new memory attached as evidence. **Revert monotonicity (R5 F4)**: snapshot restore (`restore_memory_row`) overwrites captured columns exactly, so `dream_due_version` is EXCLUDED from the restorable row — a revert preserves the row's current (monotonically higher) generation and clears `last_dreamed_at`, leaving the reverted row due; the V3 rollback path can never reintroduce a lost wakeup. **Expected-error cursor fence (R5 F5)**: `apply_dream_plan`'s expected-error fallback (`_advance_cursor` after `_EXPECTED_ACTION_ERRORS`, `src/gobby/memory/dream/apply.py:72`) is fenced identically — the selected candidate's `dream_due_version` threads into EVERY cursor-advance call including the fallback, and a mismatch stamps nothing, leaving the row due. The 1.3/1.5-before-2.2 dependency edges serialize this section's dream-service edits after theirs.

**Dream revert is a fenced, conflict-aware restore (R22 F3)**: V3 step 4 relies on `memory dream revert` as the safety rollback, yet today's `revert_dream_run` (`src/gobby/memory/dream/apply.py`) blind-upserts each snapshot's before-row through `restore_memory_row` — `RESTORE_MEMORY_SQL` (`src/gobby/memory/dream/storage.py`) overwrites the captured columns directly with no row lock, no current-state validation, and no repair intent — then invokes the index rebuild helpers per row and finally replays captured crossrefs through an independent late storage call, so a newer post-dream mutation is silently clobbered by the before-row overwrite (or races the late crossref replay), and any secondary failure leaves an active restored row with stale vector, graph, or crossref projections and no durable repair owner. The revert becomes a first-class consumer of 2.1's machinery. Per snapshot, ONE row-lock transaction: `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`, then verify the current row still matches the snapshot's AFTER-state on visibility, scope, AND every column in that snapshot's ACTION-OWNED restore set (R31 F1 — the fence and the restore operate on the same per-action column set: refresh owns `content` and `tags`, promote owns `project_id`, review/delete soft-hide owns `deleted_at` AND `dream_action` (R32 F1 — the soft-hide writes both columns in one statement and the schema couples them: `memories_dream_action_requires_deleted` CHECKs `dream_action IS NULL OR deleted_at IS NOT NULL`, so a revert that returned `deleted_at` to NULL while preserving a non-NULL `dream_action` would violate the constraint and roll the whole restore back; the fence verifies both columns against the snapshot's AFTER-state and the restore clears both to their captured before-values — NULL for a row that was active when dreamed — in the same atomic write, the canonical `restore_memory` shape); the captured before-row spans the whole memory row, so a whole-row restore behind a content/scope/visibility fence would let a tag-only or memory_type-only post-dream edit — which bumps only those columns plus `updated_at` — or a read's `access_count`/`last_accessed_at` bump pass the fence and be silently rolled back); a mismatch on any verified column means a newer post-dream mutation owns the row, so that snapshot is SKIPPED entirely — no row change, no secondary work, the skip reported as a per-id conflict in the revert result (the newer owner keeps final state everywhere). On match, the transaction restores ONLY the action-owned restore set from the before-row and PRESERVES every other captured column at its current value — `memory_type`, source fields, `access_count`, `last_accessed_at`, `graph_processed`, `created_at`, and `tags` outside refresh keep their post-dream values; `last_dreamed_at` sits outside both the fence and the restore set for every action (revert scheduling is governed by the due-generation contract: never lower `dream_due_version`, leave the row due), and `dream_action` sits outside them for refresh and promote only, whose rows stay active with `dream_action` NULL throughout; `updated_at` is never restored but stamped fresh as the revert's own write, so the reverted state wins LWW in sync export instead of losing to the dream mutation it undoes; and `dream_due_version` AND `vector_needs_reindex` stay EXCLUDED as before (generation monotonicity per the R5 F4 contract above; the intent flag has a single clearing owner per R18 F2, so a captured FALSE may never overwrite a set flag) — the transaction then stamps `vector_needs_reindex = TRUE` unconditionally as durable intent and commits, returning the row's post-restore (content, scope) scheduling snapshot. ALL post-commit secondary work — the vector upsert, graph state, pending marker, AND the captured-crossref restoration — runs as the same full fenced reconciliation with that scheduling snapshot: the late unfenced `restore_crossrefs` replay is REMOVED, and exact captured-crossref restoration executes on the held fence connection (the R19 F4 connection-aware path) only while the row still matches the restored snapshot. A revert interrupted or failed at ANY point after its primary commit leaves the stamped intent set, and the repair driver converges the row with no further mutation (repair rebuilds crossrefs from the restored content's similarity candidates — captured-crossref exactness is best-effort under failure; live-and-correct links are the guarantee). Reverting a dream-CREATED memory (before-state None) routes through ordinary delete — a recreate-safe hard-delete owner per R21 F4 — and the raw `delete_memory_row` fallback is removed: a missing row needs no delete, and leftover artifacts belong to the recurring reaper. The revert still ends with its existing post-revert reconcile pass, now exactly the repair-driver invocation that converges any stamped intent.

**Change-listener notification boundary (R10 F4)**: every direct-storage path the atomic primitive replaces notifies memory change listeners today — `mark_dreamed` calls `MemoryStoreBase._notify_listeners` after its self-owned transaction (`memories_dreams.py:47`), as do the CRUD update/rescope paths — but `MemoryDreamStore` holds only `HubDatabase`, and without a committed contract an implementation could emit no notification or emit one from a connection-aware helper before the outer transaction commits (a lie on rollback). Contract: the connection-accepting variants (mark_dreamed, snapshot insert/complete, update/rescope participants) NEVER notify inside the transaction; the non-connection variants keep today's behavior for existing callers. `MemoryStoreBase`'s public `notify_changed()` wrapper over the private `_notify_listeners` (added by 2.1 — R13 F8) is consumed here; the atomic apply primitive takes an `on_committed: Callable[[], None] | None` parameter, and the dream apply boundary passes the memory storage's `notify_changed` — invoked exactly ONCE after a successful commit, and NEVER on fence mismatch or rollback (mismatch performs nothing, so it announces nothing).

**Mark-due eligibility (E2, deterministic — no new threshold knob)**: exactly the older memories that survive P1's fused, scope-filtered `related_evidence_top_k` result (vector floor included) for the newly created memory. Reusing the fused RRF ranks keeps common-word matches from repeatedly rescheduling unrelated memories.

**Acceptance:**

- 2.2.1 - Creating a memory marks exactly the fused top_k older survivors dream-due (`last_dreamed_at = NULL`) and touches nothing else; disabled flag skips it; failures never propagate to the create path. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_auto_mark_due`.
- 2.2.2 - `mark_memories_due` updates exactly the given ids that still match the expected scope with `deleted_at IS NULL`; a listed row rescoped to another project, rescoped to global, or soft-hidden before the mark stays untouched while the remaining in-scope active rows are marked (R13 F6). test: `tests/storage/test_memories_dreams.py::test_mark_memories_due`.
- 2.2.3 - Positive coverage: keyword-only sandwich-pair shape and vector-only strong-similarity shape both mark due. Negative coverage: common-word overlap, cross-scope rows, and outside-top_k hits do not mark due. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_mark_due_eligibility_boundary`.
- 2.2.4 - Write-path scope boundaries: a project-scoped create marks due only rows in that project (never global or other-project rows); a global create marks due only global rows. Retrieval-to-update interleaving (R13 F6): the background invocation paused after evidence retrieval, then one hydrated target rescoped and another soft-hidden — the resumed mark touches neither row while still-in-scope active targets are marked. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_mark_due_write_scope`.
- 2.2.5 - Outcome gating: `created` and `reactivated` outcomes each schedule mark-due; a `deduped` outcome schedules nothing and leaves every older memory's `last_dreamed_at` unchanged. Reactivation anchor regression (R6 F5): a memory created long ago, hidden, then reactivated after an intervening related memory was created — the intervening memory (older than the reactivation, newer than the original creation) IS marked due. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_mark_due_dedup_noop`. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_mark_due_reactivation_anchor`.
- 2.2.6 - Interleaving guard, parametrized over every mutating action (keep cursor-stamp, review soft-hide, delete soft-hide, refresh, promote): a sweep paused after candidate selection, then a concurrent mark-due, a concurrent content/tag update, a concurrent rescope (project change), or a concurrent soft-hide (each exercised separately, R6 F6) — the resumed action leaves the row completely unmutated — no snapshot row, no content/tag/scope change, no stamp, and no secondary-index effect (and for the mark-due case the row stays due); with no concurrent mark-due each action applies normally with its snapshot completed and (for refresh/promote) its secondary-index reconciliation running after commit; an injected mid-transaction failure rolls the fenced transaction back leaving no snapshot row and no mutation; fresh-create and migration paths agree on the `dream_due_version` column. Listener boundary (R10 F4): a registered change listener fires exactly once after a successful fenced apply commits, and never fires on fence mismatch or on the injected rollback. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_mark_due_lost_wakeup_guard`. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_atomic_apply_listener_boundary`. test: `tests/storage/test_migration_contract.py`.
- 2.2.7 - Fence completeness (R5 F4/F5) and fenced revert (R22 F3): a post-apply mark-due followed by `memory_dream_revert` never lowers `dream_due_version` and leaves the row due; and the expected-error path — action paused, concurrent mark-due, forced expected action error, resumed fallback cursor-advance — leaves `last_dreamed_at` NULL with the incremented version intact. Revert conflict and convergence (R22 F3): a memory refreshed by a dream run and then manually updated AFTER the run — the revert SKIPS that row (reported as a conflict) and the manual update's content, vector, and crossrefs survive in every store; an unmutated row reverts with its captured crossrefs restored on the fence connection and NO late unfenced replay observable; a revert whose fenced reconciliation is induced to fail after the primary restore commits leaves the stamped intent set and the repair driver converges vector, graph, pending marker, and crossrefs with no further mutation; a captured-`vector_needs_reindex`-FALSE before-row never clears a set flag on restore; and reverting a dream-created memory routes through ordinary delete with its recreate-safe cleanup. Action-owned restore set (R31 F1): a dream-refreshed memory whose tags are edited AFTER the run is SKIPPED by the revert as a conflict (tags are refresh-owned; the post-dream tag edit survives in every store); a dream-promoted memory whose tags are edited after the run reverts its scope while the tag edit survives (tags sit outside promote's restore set); post-dream `access_count`/`last_accessed_at` bumps and a memory_type edit survive any accepted revert unchanged (no operational counter or unowned column is ever restored); and an accepted revert's `updated_at` is freshly stamped, never the captured before-value. Soft-hide revert schema coupling (R32 F1): against the real `memories_dream_action_requires_deleted` constraint, a review-hidden and a delete-hidden memory each revert to an active row with `deleted_at` NULL and `dream_action` NULL in one atomic write — never a two-step that transits the illegal `deleted_at` NULL with `dream_action` non-NULL state — and a soft-hidden row manually restored before the revert runs is SKIPPED as a conflict because the fence compares both owned columns to the snapshot's AFTER-state. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_dream_revert_action_owned_restore_set`. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_dream_revert_preserves_due_generation`. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_expected_error_cursor_fence`. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_dream_revert_conflict_skips_newer_mutation`. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_dream_revert_failure_converges`.
- 2.2.8 - Burst bound (R9 F2): with retrieval blocked and a burst of creates larger than `WRITE_MARK_DUE_MAX_CONCURRENCY`, concurrently active `RelatedEvidenceSession` objects never exceed the cap and concurrently open evidence DB connections never exceed the cap times the page semaphore; queued invocations start only as earlier ones release the semaphore and all complete after unblocking; every started invocation's session is closed in `finally` (none leak on failure). Shutdown drain (R10 F3, R11 F3, R12 F2, R13 F1, R15 F1): invoking the REAL `MemoryManager.close` with TWO active mark-due tasks whose DB calls are genuinely blocked against a wedged endpoint (including one blocked on its FIRST `SET LOCAL` round trip, before any timeout is installed), multiple semaphore-queued tasks, AND a background dedup task blocked mid-request against a trickling remote stub cancels and awaits the whole set before secondary-resource teardown — queued tasks never construct a session, both sessions' `aclose` drains run concurrently (a sequential 2×7s drain would exceed the budget; the test's wall-clock bound proves concurrency), the dedup task's cancellation aborts its in-flight Qdrant request (the stub records zero request activity after close), every blocked DB call terminates via 0.3's terminal sequence with zero DB/stub activity after close (R15 F1, R16 F1 — no cancel_safe escalation, no abandonment), `_background_tasks` drains to empty, every dedicated evidence connection is closed, no mark-due task, connection, or thread exists after close returns (no thread is ever created — thread accounting), secondary teardown (including the async client close) starts only after every task terminates, and close completes inside the raised 10s runner budget. Local-mode dedup skip (R13 F1): with a path-mode vector store, a burst of creates schedules ZERO background dedup tasks (and no Qdrant worker of any kind), the once-per-process warning logs exactly once, and the creates' storage writes and foreground indexing still complete. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_mark_due_burst_bounded`. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_manager_close_drains_mark_due`. test: `tests/memory/test_create_supersedes.py::test_local_mode_skips_background_dedup`.
- 2.2.9 - Imported new knowledge marks older memories due (R25 F2): through the real public JSONL import entry point, a fresh imported memory and a reactivating import each mark exactly the fused in-scope OLDER survivors due (`last_dreamed_at = NULL` with the version increment) using the outcome-specific anchor — a reactivating import wakes a memory created between the row's original creation and its reactivation — while `updated` (winning LWW replace), `unchanged` (losing or no-op), and `deduped` imports leave every older memory's `last_dreamed_at` untouched; an import burst larger than `WRITE_MARK_DUE_MAX_CONCURRENCY` never exceeds the cap's concurrently active sessions; and a CLI restore returns only after its scheduled mark-due invocations complete on the call-owned loop; a cancelled import whose primary committed a `created` or `reactivated` outcome still marks the fused older survivors due before the cancelled public await returns REGARDLESS of where in that record cancellation lands — during the primary drain, during the fenced secondary reconciliation, before hook ownership is established, while the hook is in flight, or in an indeterminate-commit window resolved by the bounded state-verification read to an applied or conservatively-owing outcome (R29 F1) — the owed hook is drained exactly once under the same shielded-ownership contract as the primary (2.1.25, R27 F1, R28 F1) because a missed mark-due wakeup has no durable intent and the recurring repair driver cannot recreate it. test: `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py::test_import_created_reactivated_marks_older_due`. test: `tests/sync/test_memory_sync.py::test_import_updated_unchanged_deduped_no_mark_due`.

## P3: Migrate model defaults to the gpt-5.6 family
`kind: framing`

**Goal**: The gpt-5.6 family (sol/terra/luna per tier) replaces every functional `gpt-5.5` reference in code, config, templates, tests, doc-comments, user-facing guides, and the web UI (Josh's directives 2026-07-18); the plan-adversary agents already run at xhigh (satisfied prerequisite — see 3.3); the static context-window table is removed in favor of override/provider/registry resolution.

The 5.5 swap is explicitly authorized by Josh's all-references directive and deliberately overrides the never-delete-catalog-models review lesson (71319547f/#17775) — record the directive in the commit message.

### 3.4 Remove the static context-window table [category: code] (depends: 3.1, 2.2)
`kind: deliverable`

Target: `src/gobby/llm/context_windows.py`, `src/gobby/servers/provider_models.py`, `src/gobby/servers/provider_model_discovery.py`, `src/gobby/servers/provider_models_grok.py`, `src/gobby/servers/chat_session_messages.py`, `src/gobby/servers/websocket/chat/_session.py`, `src/gobby/servers/websocket/chat/backends/base.py`, `src/gobby/sessions/context_usage.py`, `src/gobby/servers/routes/sessions/lifecycle.py`, `src/gobby/servers/websocket/handlers/session_observe_proxy.py`, `src/gobby/cli/sessions.py`, `src/gobby/llm/model_registry.py`, `src/gobby/storage/model_costs.py`, `src/gobby/storage/model_metadata.py` (new, renamed), `src/gobby/runner_lifecycle_periodic.py`, `src/gobby/runner_lifecycle_shutdown.py`, `src/gobby/runner_init/storage.py`, `src/gobby/storage/postgres_baseline_schema.sql`, `src/gobby/storage/migrations/325_model_metadata_rename.sql` (new), `web/src/lib/providerModels.ts`, `web/src/lib/__tests__/providerModels.test.ts`, `tests/llm/test_context_window.py`, `tests/servers/test_provider_models.py`, `tests/servers/routes/test_providers.py`, `tests/sessions/test_context_usage.py`, `tests/servers/test_chat_session.py`, `tests/servers/test_sessions_routes.py`, `tests/servers/test_session_control.py`, `tests/cli/test_cli_sessions_coverage.py`, `tests/storage/test_model_costs.py`, `tests/storage/test_model_metadata.py` (new, renamed), `tests/test_runner_model_metadata_refresh.py` (new), `tests/llm/test_model_registry.py`, `tests/storage/test_migration_contract.py`, `tests/storage/test_migration_runner.py`

Static model-metadata tables rot — this plan exists because a stale record survived, and the table already lacks the new default model. The durable cache Josh asked for already exists: OpenRouter model data persists to Postgres via `ModelCostStore` (`model_costs` table, prefix matching; `src/gobby/llm/model_registry.py::lookup_context_window`). Changes (R2 F6 — this section exclusively owns every file listed above; 3.1's sweep excludes them):

- Delete the static context-length table and `static_context_length_for_model` from `src/gobby/llm/context_windows.py`, repairing every consumer: `src/gobby/servers/provider_models.py` imports and calls it (lines 25, 78, 100 — the `ContextLengthCandidate` static branch), and the `static_default` source label it feeds is also emitted by `src/gobby/servers/provider_model_discovery.py:489` and `src/gobby/servers/provider_models_grok.py:18,27` — the label retires with the table and those producers move to the new source labels.
- `resolve_context_window_with_source` resolution becomes: **explicit user override first** → provider-reported/local (ACP, codex app-server runtime metadata) → provider catalog → DB-backed registry (`ModelCostStore`) → **explicit unknown**, with a loud once-per-model warning. No sentinel values — a guessed number (e.g. 1M) misrepresents usage worse than no number (Josh confirmed: no 1M fallback).
- **Override plumbing (R6 F7)**: the resolver ALREADY applies its explicit `overrides` argument as the highest-precedence branch with source `"override"` — that argument stays THE injection contract (the resolver has no AppConfig access and gains none). The `src/gobby/servers/websocket/chat/_session.py:503` assignment of `daemon_cfg.context_window_overrides` onto `session._context_window_overrides` is RETAINED — it is how the chat callers (`src/gobby/servers/chat_session_messages.py:239,338`, `src/gobby/servers/websocket/chat/backends/base.py:238`) feed the resolver today, and deleting it would silently drop chat overrides. The actual gap is consumers that never receive overrides: the resolver calls in `src/gobby/sessions/context_usage.py` (:116–135) gain an explicit `overrides` parameter threaded from their daemon-config-holding callers, so `context_window_overrides` wins uniformly across chat and context-usage surfaces. **Terminal-surface callers (R7 F4)**: the concrete override-less consumers are `effective_context_window_for_session` and `backfill_session_context_windows` — both gain a keyword-only `overrides` parameter and check the session's effective model against it FIRST, before the live/event/reported/model-resolution ladder (consistent with the committed override-first resolution order). Their callers thread the config they already hold: `sessions_get` (`src/gobby/servers/routes/sessions/lifecycle.py:152`) passes the daemon config's `context_window_overrides` (routes reach it via the server's services container); `handle_attach_to_session` (`src/gobby/servers/websocket/handlers/session_observe_proxy.py:100`) passes it from the mixin's `daemon_config` attribute (already declared on `SessionControlMixin`); and the CLI backfill command (`backfill_context_windows`, `src/gobby/cli/sessions.py:731`) loads the daemon config and passes its overrides into `backfill_session_context_windows`.
- **Explicit-unknown typed shape (R6 F8)**: today `ResolvedContextWindow.value` is `int`, its source literal has no `"unknown"`, and the resolver returns bare `None` on a miss — which cannot carry a source. Commit: `ResolvedContextWindow.value` becomes `int | None` and the source literal gains `"unknown"`; `resolve_context_window_with_source` returns `ResolvedContextWindow(None, "unknown")` for a nonempty unknown model (bare `None` remains only for empty/absent model input); `resolve_context_window` stays the `int | None` convenience wrapper (unwraps `value`); provider-model serialization emits `context_length: null` plus `context_length_source: "unknown"`; every truthiness/value consumer of the resolved object is updated to test `value is not None`.
- **Frontend consumer (R11 F5)**: the web validator `isProviderModelOption` (`web/src/lib/providerModels.ts:86`) whitelists `context_length_source` against exactly `provider_reported | provider_catalog | registry | static_default` — it still accepts the retired `static_default` and lacks `unknown`, so provider entries serialized as `context_length: null` / `context_length_source: "unknown"` would fail the type guard and be discarded. The whitelist becomes exactly the new source-label set (`override`, `provider_reported`, `provider_catalog`, `registry`, `unknown`); `static_default` is rejected (Rule 16 — no backward compat). The web suite asserts a `null`-window `unknown`-source catalog entry survives fetch/validation and a `static_default` entry no longer does. This makes 3.4 a fullstack deliverable — its manifest entry carries `implementation_domain: fullstack`.
- **Registry cache rename + freshness (R3 F4, Josh's directive)**: cost tracking is retired — the table stores only context-window metadata (no pricing columns exist), so the misnomer goes with this work: rename table `model_costs` → `model_metadata` (baseline schema plus migration `325_model_metadata_rename.sql` — consecutive after 2.2's 324; the `(depends: 3.1, 2.2)` edge serializes the shared baseline-schema and migration-contract edits, and 3.4 is their final owner — R5 F6; covered by the migration contract/runner tests). **Constraint-name parity (R6 F9)**: `ALTER TABLE ... RENAME TO` keeps the primary-key constraint named `model_costs_pkey` (migration 311 created it explicitly), while a fresh baseline `CREATE TABLE model_metadata` mints `model_metadata_pkey` — so migration 325 must ALSO run an existence-guarded `ALTER TABLE model_metadata RENAME CONSTRAINT model_costs_pkey TO model_metadata_pkey` after the table rename, leaving fresh-create and upgraded catalogs byte-identical in `pg_constraint` and `ModelCostStore` → `ModelMetadataStore` (`src/gobby/storage/model_costs.py` → `src/gobby/storage/model_metadata.py`; consumers `src/gobby/llm/model_registry.py` and `src/gobby/runner_init/storage.py` updated; the storage test file follows the rename). Freshness marker: the EXISTING `updated_at` column (`DEFAULT NOW()`) — `populate()` deletes and reinserts, so every row's `updated_at` is the last successful fetch time, and an empty/failed fetch keeps old rows whose old timestamps signal staleness. No new column, no `fetched_at`.
- **Refresh lifecycle (R3 F5, R5 F7, R11 F4)**: the 24h refresh registers in `src/gobby/runner_lifecycle_periodic.py` (`start_periodic_tasks`, alongside the existing periodic jobs) AND in the shutdown path — `src/gobby/runner_lifecycle_shutdown.py::_cancel_periodic_tasks` (:343) cancels only the attributes in its hardcoded `periodic_task_attrs` tuple, so `_model_metadata_refresh_task` is added to that tuple; registering the task only in `start_periodic_tasks` would leave it outside daemon shutdown cancellation. **Cancellation must reach the work, not just the task (R11 F4)**: `_cancel_runner_task` waits only 2s while `fetch_models_sync` permits a 10s HTTP call (`_FETCH_TIMEOUT = 10.0`), so offloading the whole synchronous `populate()` via `asyncio.to_thread` would leave a default-executor worker fetching — and then writing to the database — after shutdown cancellation "succeeded", racing storage teardown. The periodic job is therefore split at the cancellation boundary: the network fetch runs as cancellable async I/O — new `fetch_models_async` in `src/gobby/llm/model_registry.py` (httpx `AsyncClient`; response parsing factored into a shared helper used by both fetch variants) — so task cancellation interrupts at the await; the DB write phase is async as well (R15 F1) — no executor or thread exists anywhere in the refresh job, and at most one delete-and-reinsert transaction is ever in flight. **The write must be bounded end-to-end, not assumed short (R12 F6, async-native R15 F1)**: `populate`'s sync transaction cannot be preempted from the event loop, and offloading it to a thread would recreate the unterminatable-worker class R15 F1 eliminated — a worker abandoned on cancel failure still owns a live connection that can resume or roll back AFTER storage teardown, contradicting 3.4.5's zero-post-teardown-DB-activity contract. The refresh write (the renamed store gains an ASYNC bounded write form used by the periodic job, models pre-fetched; startup `populate()` unchanged) runs through 0.3's `run_bounded_db` on a dedicated async connection under ONE absolute monotonic deadline, module constant `MODEL_METADATA_WRITE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 5.0` — `statement_timeout` is per STATEMENT, so a single `SET LOCAL` cannot bound a multi-statement transaction (R13 F3): connect draws down the budget, and the write is exactly FOUR statements on that connection — `SET LOCAL statement_timeout` at the post-connect remainder, the DELETE, a second `SET LOCAL` re-installing the recomputed remainder, then ONE multi-row `INSERT ... VALUES` (`executemany` is retired for this write: each of its parameter sets is a separate statement that would reset the timeout, R13 F3; the re-install is itself a statement, so the count is four, corrected R14 F2) — with an explicit deadline check before each statement AND before COMMIT (a nonpositive remainder rolls back without issuing it; an expiry after COMMIT submission surfaces 0.3's typed indeterminate-commit outcome, benign here because the write is a full delete-and-reinsert the next refresh cycle reconciles, R16 F2), so cumulative time is bounded cooperatively by the deadline and unconditionally by 0.3's termination contract. Shutdown runs ONE monotonic deadline of `MODEL_METADATA_DRAIN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 7.0` (write deadline + 2s margin): `_cancel_runner_task` cancels the job task — psycopg's cancellation handler makes its own bounded server-cancel attempt — and if the task has not terminated after a short grace, 0.3's two-step terminal sequence runs — the job's registered live connection is hard-closed locally and the task cancelled a second time (R16 F1), failing any main-socket wait immediately and interrupting psycopg's separate cancel-connection wait — INCLUDING the first `SET LOCAL` that no installed timeout bounds (R13 F3) and the case where the server-side cancel request itself blocks or fails (R14 F2, closed R15 F1/R16 F1): the server aborts the uncommitted transaction on EOF, nothing is abandoned, no thread exists, and the drain returns within its deadline. The startup path keeps synchronous `populate()` and `fetch_models_sync` remains for it. Warnings: refresh failure retains cached rows and logs; serving rows older than 48h warns once. Startup behavior in `src/gobby/runner_init/storage.py` is unchanged apart from the rename.
- **Shared route-test ownership (R3 F6)**: `tests/servers/routes/test_providers.py` is edited by both 3.1 (catalog entries) and this section — the `context_length_source` label assertions (lines 222 and 358) retire with `static_default` and belong to 3.4. The `(depends: 3.1)` edge serializes the edits; 3.4 is the final owner of the label assertions.
- Fix ratio consumers to guard unknown at the division site: no percentage when the window is unknown (show raw tokens), no divide-by-zero. Callers that genuinely require a window apply an explicit documented policy at their own call site.
- Existing coverage lives in `tests/llm/test_context_window.py` (singular — it imports the deleted function and asserts the retired `static_default` source) and `tests/servers/test_provider_models.py`; both are updated in place. No new plural-named test file is created.

**Acceptance:**

- 3.4.1 - The static table is gone; resolution order is override → provider-reported → provider catalog → registry → explicit unknown with a once-per-model warning; a nonempty unknown model yields `ResolvedContextWindow(None, "unknown")` while `resolve_context_window` returns plain `None` for it (both surfaces tested directly, R6 F8); resolver tests cover an explicit override winning over every other source (source `"override"`), `gpt-5.6-sol`, `gpt-5.6-terra`, and `gpt-5.6-luna` (bare and `codex/`-qualified) resolving via the registry, and provider-model serialization emitting `context_length: null` + `context_length_source: "unknown"` (never a sentinel). test: `tests/llm/test_context_window.py`. symbol: `gobby.llm.context_windows.resolve_context_window_with_source`.
- 3.4.2 - Context-usage ratio consumers handle unknown windows without division errors and without fabricated percentages. test: `tests/llm/test_context_window.py::test_unknown_window_consumers_guard`.
- 3.4.8 - Override plumbing (R6 F7): chat sessions still honor `context_window_overrides` end-to-end (the `_session.py` assignment feeds the chat resolver calls), and the `src/gobby/sessions/context_usage.py` resolution helpers apply an explicitly threaded `overrides` mapping ahead of every other source. Terminal surfaces (R7 F4): a configured override for the session's model wins over catalog/registry resolution at each public surface — the `sessions_get` route payload's `context_window`, the `attach_to_session` websocket payload's context window, and the CLI backfill's resolved window each reflect the override. test: `tests/sessions/test_context_usage.py::test_context_window_overrides_applied`. test: `tests/servers/test_chat_session.py`. test: `tests/servers/test_sessions_routes.py::test_sessions_get_context_window_override`. test: `tests/servers/test_session_control.py::test_attach_context_window_override`. test: `tests/cli/test_cli_sessions_coverage.py::test_backfill_context_windows_override`.
- 3.4.3 - All `static_default` producers and consumers are repaired: provider-model candidate chains and discovery/grok source labels use the new resolution sources; no import of the deleted function remains. test: `tests/servers/test_provider_models.py`.
- 3.4.4 - Rename + freshness: table `model_metadata` and `ModelMetadataStore` land everywhere (no `model_costs` identifier remains outside migration history); freshness is read from the existing `updated_at` (delete-and-reinsert stamps it; failed/empty fetch retains old rows and their old timestamps); refresh failure warns while keeping cached rows; stale (>48h) rows warn once. test: `tests/storage/test_model_metadata.py`. test: `tests/llm/test_model_registry.py`.
- 3.4.5 - Refresh lifecycle: the periodic job registers in `start_periodic_tasks` on a 24h cadence with `_model_metadata_refresh_task` in the `_cancel_periodic_tasks` tuple; the fetch AND write phases are cancellable async I/O — no refresh executor or thread exists (R11 F4, R15 F1). The test invokes the real shutdown helper twice: once with the refresh blocked mid-fetch, proving cancellation interrupts the fetch and no database access survives the helper, and once with a DB write in flight, proving the write terminates (committed or rolled back) before storage teardown. Bounded-write regressions (R12 F6, R13 F3, R14 F2, R15 F1): five shapes — the connect phase blocked (endpoint accepts TCP, never completes); a server-side-blocked statement; a DELETE and INSERT each individually under the installed timeout whose sum exceeds the write deadline (the recomputed remainder must stop the INSERT — per-statement resets never extend the write); the FIRST `SET LOCAL` round trip blocked, before any timeout is installed; and the server-side cancellation request ITSELF blocked or failing — EVERY shape terminates within `MODEL_METADATA_WRITE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (the shutdown-path shapes within `MODEL_METADATA_DRAIN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`) via 0.3's terminal sequence — pre-COMMIT expiry rolls the transaction back, while an expiry after COMMIT submission surfaces the typed indeterminate-commit outcome, logged and reconciled by the next cycle's delete-and-reinsert (R16 F2) — nothing abandoned, no thread ever created, the event loop responsive throughout, and zero database access after storage teardown (R5 F7, R11 F4, R12 F6, R13 F3, R14 F2, R15 F1). test: `tests/test_runner_model_metadata_refresh.py`.
- 3.4.6 - Migration correctness: fresh-create (baseline) and upgrade (rename migration) paths agree on the `model_metadata` shape INCLUDING constraint names — a `pg_constraint` inspection proves both paths yield the same table name, primary-key constraint name (`model_metadata_pkey`), and key columns, with no `model_costs_pkey` surviving upgrade (R6 F9); migration contract and runner tests pass. test: `tests/storage/test_migration_contract.py`. test: `tests/storage/test_migration_runner.py`.
- 3.4.7 - Shared route test: the `context_length_source` assertions in the providers route test reflect the new source labels, edited only after 3.1's catalog changes land. test: `tests/servers/routes/test_providers.py`.
- 3.4.9 - Frontend contract (R11 F5): the web validator accepts exactly the new source-label set including `unknown`, a `context_length: null` + `context_length_source: "unknown"` entry survives validation, `static_default` is rejected, and the targeted web test passes: `cd web && npx vitest run src/lib/__tests__/providerModels.test.ts`. file: `web/src/lib/providerModels.ts`.

## P4: Plan-Workflow Feedback Presentation
`kind: framing`

**Goal**: Coordinators surface agent review feedback to the human verbatim and collect per-item votes — the human is the scope gate and needs the raw text (Josh, 2026-07-17, during this plan's enhancement round).

### 4.1 Amend plan skill to require verbatim per-item feedback presentation [category: docs]
`kind: deliverable`

Target: `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/plan/SKILL.md`, `tests/skills/test_plan_skill_delegated_mode.py`

The skill moved under this plan (R10 F7): commit `4d4ca36e7` (2026-07-18) inserted a mandatory `compact_self` call as step 4.5 substep 2 — and an equivalent mandatory post-launch compact in step 5 — shifting enhancement presentation to step 4.5 substep 3; adversary results are read and recorded in step 6, and revision happens in step 7. Amend step 4.5 **substep 3** (enhancement presentation) and step **6** (the adversary presentation/vote gate: after result receipt and changelog append, before step 7's revision), preserving BOTH mandatory compact_self launch steps verbatim, so the coordinator MUST:

1. Present a ranked summary of all suggestions/findings (id, lens/severity, location, impact/effort/risk, one-line gist).
2. Present each item's **full text verbatim** (description + suggested enhancement/finding detail + metadata) — never only compressed summaries.
3. When an item modifies existing plan text, quote the current plan sections it touches so the human can judge without opening the file.
4. Collect an individual accept/decline vote per item (not one bulk vote), supporting per-item exploration before voting.
5. Put the full item text inside the interaction payload itself (e.g. the question text of the vote prompt) — coordinator harnesses may drop free text emitted between tool calls, so text outside the payload is not guaranteed to render.

Record declined-with-deferral items as typed `kind: deferred` sections pointing at the follow-up task (this plan's 2.3 is the worked example).

The existing skill-contract suite `tests/skills/test_plan_skill_delegated_mode.py` already pins this contract family (artifact-first, taskless spawn shape, changelog format, manifest handoff — plus, since `4d4ca36e7`, the mandatory-compaction contract for both launch steps); it gains focused assertions for the five presentation requirements in BOTH amended steps, with the full-item-text-inside-the-interaction-payload requirement asserted explicitly (R8 F6), and the pre-existing compaction assertions must still pass unmodified (R10 F7 — the amendment may not displace either compact_self step).

**Acceptance:**

- 4.1.1 - Plan skill step 4.5 substep 3 requires ranked summary + full verbatim per-suggestion text + quoted current plan excerpts + per-item votes inside the interaction payload, while substep 2's mandatory compact_self text is preserved verbatim (R10 F7). file: `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/plan/SKILL.md`. test: `tests/skills/test_plan_skill_delegated_mode.py::test_enhancement_presentation_contract`.
- 4.1.2 - Plan skill step 6 applies the same verbatim presentation contract to adversary findings — the vote gate sits after result receipt and changelog append and before step 7's revision — and step 5's mandatory post-launch compact_self text is preserved verbatim (R10 F7). file: `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/plan/SKILL.md`. test: `tests/skills/test_plan_skill_delegated_mode.py::test_adversary_presentation_contract`.

## V3 Post-Build Ops Runbook
`kind: verification`

Machine-local reconciliation, executed by the operator (or coordinator with Josh present) ONLY after all development leaves are closed with validated commits — not an expansion deliverable (F9). Commands below are the real CLI forms (R2 F8): `gobby memory dream` runs synchronously with `--timeout` (there is no `--wait`), the live flag is `--full`, and subcommands are `gobby memory dream status <run-id>` / `gobby memory dream revert <run-id>`. Gated sequence (F10):

1. Install the rebuilt Rust binaries from 3.2's commits into `~/.gobby/bin/{gcode,gwiki}` per the workspace conventions in `crates/CLAUDE.md`, then restart the daemon with `memory.dream.allow_unattended_mutations` still **disabled**. The restart runs bundled drift sync; read back the installed plan-adversary registry rows and confirm both carry `reasoning_effort: xhigh` — verifying the pre-existing committed state from `8fade8961` (R9 F4, R10 F6 — machine-local install, restart, and installed-row readback live here; 3.3 produces no expansion leaf).
2. Run `uv run gobby memory dream --dry-run --timeout 900`. Gate: zero planner errors AND the plan shows evidence-cited verdicts (including the f0652bc1/ee3b9e52 regression-pair shape) AND Josh reviews the complete proposed action set — every eligible page, every action (1.3's full-coverage dry-run pagination, R9 F1) — via `uv run gobby memory dream status <run-id>`. Record the run id.
3. Only if every gate passes: set `memory.dream.allow_unattended_mutations = true` via gobby-config (code default stays `False`). Config saves report `requires_restart=true` — so **restart the daemon** (`uv run gobby restart`), read back the effective installed value via gobby-config and confirm it is `true`, and only then run `uv run gobby memory dream --full --timeout 1800` (live). Record the live run id and snapshot count; the exact revert command is `uv run gobby memory dream revert <run-id>`.
4. Rollback after any post-enable failure or bad live outcome: set the flag back to `false` via gobby-config, `uv run gobby restart`, read back and confirm `false`; if live mutations were bad, run `uv run gobby memory dream revert <run-id>` for the recorded live run. Any failed gate before step 3 simply leaves the flag disabled and aborts. Soft-hides carry 30/90-day purge grace either way.

  - kg-worker-failure-path-bypasses-hidden-undo / blocking / the R15 undo hung off conditional `mark_graph_processed`, but the worker (sessions/lifecycle.py:212-286) calls it only for success/noop while `add_to_graph` can write (`made_progress`) then return `partial_failure` or a connection failure — hidden-row artifacts would survive every failure path; fixed: the worker runs the post-add visibility decision and fenced undo on EVERY possibly-written path (success, partial failure, made-progress connection failure, mid-add exception) BEFORE persisting processed/failure/retry state, with a partial-write-then-failure regression — ACCEPTED (2.1, 2.1.17)

F2 is protocol ground truth (commit response loss is indeterminate; no client action can undo an applied commit). F3 confirmed at services/lifecycle.py:349-393 (graph removal, crossref delete, optional rebuild all outside the R15 fence). F4 confirmed at sessions/lifecycle.py:212-286 (`mark_graph_processed` only on success/noop) and in `KnowledgeGraphService.add_to_graph` (`partial_errors` → `partial_failure` and `_connection_failure_result(made_progress=...)` both returned after writes). At pass start the replay pins the live sync file via hard link (O(1); byte-copy fallback under the same deadline) — safe because every export replaces the file atomically via `atomic_write_text` (mkstemp + fsync + `os.replace`, `src/gobby/sync/jsonl_io.py:49-68`) under `export_file_lock` (`memories.py:781`), so the pinned inode is immutable. Continuation state becomes a byte-offset cursor into the snapshot (atomic sidecar, written at cycle end and deadline interruption) that advances past every fully-processed line — applied tombstone, LWW loser, absent-row no-op, and non-tombstone skip alike — making pure scan progress durable: a deadline may expire at any line boundary with zero skips and zero re-scan, dissolving the discovery-scan gap. Byte offsets are safe here for the reason R33 rejected them elsewhere: they target the immutable snapshot, never the replaceable live file. Pass end unlinks the snapshot and clears the cursor; the next pass pins the then-current file, preserving the wrap-around guarantee (mid-pass replacements are visited next pass; staleness bounded by one pass). Corruption of either sidecar degrades to a fresh re-pin — replay idempotence keeps both fairness devices, never correctness dependencies. Progress: offset strictly increases every cycle; ceil(T/cap) application-bound cycles, at most L scan-bound cycles pathologically, every cycle within deadline. 2.1.25 rewritten per required resolution: large mixed exporter-ordered file, deadline forced to expire mid-scan before the actionable tombstone, daemon restart resuming from cursor + snapshot, mid-pass atomic replacement served from the pinned snapshot with the new tombstone caught next pass, convergence with no record skipped (`test_tombstone_replay_multi_page_progress` extended). The rewrite's classification parse is now capped by `TOMBSTONE_REPLAY_MAX_FOREIGN_LINE_BYTES` (named module constant, default 8 MiB, 128x the tombstone cap): over-cap lines within the ceiling accumulate across scan chunks with the deadline honored at every chunk boundary and are classified with one ceiling-bounded parse (tombstones re-emit canonically; everything else, malformed JSON included, passes through byte-for-byte); a line exceeding the ceiling fails the cycle visibly with no cursor advance and no silent skip, with the documented remediation that an explicit export stamps its post-replace generation trusted (R38 stamp), after which the replay skips the oversized line unparsed under the trusted invariant. The mandatory work-unit bound is restated as the largest of the chunk size, the tombstone line cap, and the foreign-line ceiling; self-healing holds for every untrusted generation whose lines fit within the ceiling and degrades to a visible operator-recoverable failure beyond it. Acceptance 2.1.25 extended with the multi-chunk multi-window padded-tombstone arm (every cycle bounded, no allocation beyond the ceiling, final publication applies the committed delete) and the over-ceiling visible-failure arm, with companion tests `test_replay_multiwindow_padded_tombstone_stays_bounded` and `test_replay_untrusted_line_over_foreign_ceiling_fails_visibly`.   - r42-sync-publication-toctou / blocking / The R41 content-bound trust fix left a write-side TOCTOU in the replay's canonicalization publication: the publication re-verify (chunked re-hash of the live file) followed by `atomic_write_text`'s unconditional `os.replace` (`src/gobby/sync/jsonl_io.py:49-68`) is check-then-act, the export lock is an advisory flock on a sidecar real writers bypass (`src/gobby/sync/jsonl_io.py:23-30`), and worktree merge resolution overwrites `.gobby/memories.jsonl` via `git checkout --theirs` with no lock participation (`src/gobby/worktrees/merge/resolver.py:32-37`, `:69-81`) — so a foreign generation arriving after the final re-hash and before the replace would be overwritten by a transform of the older pinned snapshot, losing newly arrived tombstones and records. ACCEPTED.
- resolution_notes: All four citations verified before accepting — `atomic_write_text` (`src/gobby/sync/jsonl_io.py:49-68`) is mkstemp + fsync + unconditional `os.replace` with no conditional semantics; the other three were verified in Round 41. The race is structural: POSIX offers no compare-and-swap on a path, so no re-hash-then-replace protocol can close the window against writers that bypass the advisory flock. Fixed via the adversary's simplification branch — the replay-owned canonicalization rewrite is retired entirely and the replay is now non-mutating: over-cap lines in an untrusted generation are parsed in place within the R39 foreign-line ceiling (the accumulation, chunking, deadline, and restart machinery survives unchanged) and a parsed tombstone applies its committed delete through the identical application path as an under-cap tombstone, with non-tombstone and malformed lines passed over; the replay performs zero writes to the sync file path — no temp, no export-lock acquisition, no `os.replace`, no replay-written trust stamp — eliminating the observation-to-publication race class rather than narrowing its window. This is strictly less mechanism: after R39 every over-cap line was already being accumulated and parsed for classification during the rewrite, so re-emitting it canonically bought only amortization of future passes at the cost of the temp/checkpoint/LOCK_NB-publication machinery and the data-loss race. The file heals to canonical form at the next routine or explicit export (which under R36 canonicalizes every tombstone it emits and stamps trust through the two-exit R40/R41 content-bound stamp — now the sole trust writer); until then each periodic pass re-parses the untrusted generation's over-cap lines, bounded per-pass and harmless to repeat because tombstone application is idempotent under the R31/R32 arm state machine. A replay-side trust stamp after an all-non-tombstone pass was considered and rejected as unneeded mechanism: over-cap non-tombstone records are rare, exports run on every memory change, and stamping after a pass that applied tombstones would falsify the trusted-skip invariant for the reaper's re-reconciliation. Over-ceiling behavior, remediation, and the trust-slot persistence semantics are unchanged. Acceptance 2.1.25 reworked: the legacy-first-cycle arm now proves parse-and-apply with the live file left byte-identical; the R38 rewrite-path arms are replaced by a sync-file-read-only arm and the adversary's required foreign-interleaving regression (an unlocked foreign/Git replacement landing at any instant during a replay pass — including immediately before the pass's final work unit — survives byte-identical and its tombstone is applied by the next pass); companion tests renamed/added: `test_tombstone_replay_untrusted_overcap_parses_and_applies_inline`, `test_replay_never_writes_sync_file`, `test_replay_overcap_parse_resumes_across_deadline_windows`, `test_foreign_replacement_during_pass_survives_replay`. Finding-count trajectory: 10 -> 8 -> 6 -> 5 -> 7 -> 9 -> 4 -> 6 -> 4 -> 7 -> 6 -> 6 -> 8 -> 5 -> 3 -> 5 -> 4 -> 2 -> 4 -> 4 -> 5 -> 3 -> 1 -> 1 -> 2 -> 1 -> 1 -> 1 -> 1 -> 2 -> 2 -> 2 -> 2 -> 1 -> 1 -> 1 -> 1 -> 2 -> 1 -> 1 -> 1 -> 1.

PARTIALLY ACCEPTED: the claimed enforcement mechanism was refuted — `uv run gobby plans validate` passed on the pre-fix artifact because `_mentioned_paths` counts acceptance refs only for artifact kinds file/behavior (test refs exempt, `src/gobby/plans/semantic_lint.py:292-296`) and body mentions only behind an intent-verb gate that excludes 3.2's "become" phrasing (`src/gobby/plans/semantic_lint.py:27-31`), and `docs/contracts/plan-coverage.md` defines no target-coverage rule; the inventory-completeness substance was applied in full.
- resolution_notes: All three findings verified against code before acting. F1: `auto_resolve_trivial_conflicts` confirmed running `git checkout --theirs` with no lock participation (`src/gobby/worktrees/merge/resolver.py:40-111`), and both R34/R35 pin mechanisms shared the false premise that a pinned inode means immutable bytes. Resolved by making the snapshot genuinely byte-immutable rather than detecting mutation mid-traversal: acquisition now streams a private distinct-inode snapshot copy (replay-owned, beside the traversal sidecar) under the existing chunk/deadline/restart machinery while accumulating a SHA-256, then proves source stability by re-hashing the live file and comparing digests — equality proves the copy captured a complete generation that existed; any mid-acquisition write, replacement or in-place, forces discard-and-re-acquire, so no tombstone from a mixed generation is ever applied and unstable bytes are never traversed at all, in any generation. The verified digest doubles as the generation identity, so the trust verdict is decided once at verification time and R41's lazy running-digest/read-ahead machinery is retired as unnecessary mechanism; the copy is digest-re-verified on restart resume; traversal, cursor offsets, R39 accumulation, and R42 parse-and-apply run unchanged over the verified copy. Retired with rationale kept as history: R34's hard-link pin and R35's descriptor-pin fallback with its no-byte-copy premise — the copy is now the mechanism, the per-pass price of byte-immutability under non-cooperating in-place writers. The residual window (an in-place writer pausing across the entire copy-plus-verification span while leaving torn bytes that form a valid canonical tombstone line for an active id) is documented as the irreducible residue of any read protocol under a non-cooperating writer. F2: resolved via the adversary's simplest branch — physically separate single-writer sidecars, an exporter-owned trust sidecar and a replay-owned traversal sidecar; ownership rather than locking removes the lost-update class, per-file atomic replacement then suffices, and trust persistence across pass completion and degradation becomes structural. Both write interleavings added as acceptance arms. F3: the sweep ran with the linter's own parser (parse_plan + collect_target_inventory + normalize_file_path) across every deliverable section; acceptance-test artifacts added to Targets in 0.1, 0.3, 1.1, 1.2, 1.3, 2.1, 2.2, 3.4, and 5.1, the three body-edited Rust files added to 3.2's Target, and the one partial prose path canonicalized (3.4's `sessions/context_usage.py` to `src/gobby/sessions/context_usage.py`). Deliberately not added, as pure reference citations rather than edit artifacts: the `postgres_baseline_schema.sql:759` evidence cite in 1.1, the namespace-package `__init__.py` note in 2.1, the `memories_crud.py` reactivation-branch cite in 2.2, and the `cd web && npx vitest run` command arguments in 3.4/3.5 whose web-relative segments are not repo paths, and 1.5's `src/gobby/utils/wiki_vault.py` and `wiki/_meta/truth_digest.json` cites (runtime read dependencies, not edited artifacts).     exactly-five-field tombstone made over-cap by its id OR project_id is parsed and applied

- resolution_notes: Unattended round. Claims verified before applying: the replay passage
    traverses in physical line order with non-tombstones skipped without database work and
    tombstones applied through LWW-guarded per-tombstone transactions against the current row
    only; _import_memories_from_lines canonicalizes via _deduplicate_records_by_id before any
    apply; _merge_memory_records delegates same-id tombstone collisions to newer_record
    (greater timestamp wins, tombstone on exact ties); apply_tombstone reads only the local
    row's updated_at and hard-DELETEs; memories.id is UUID PRIMARY KEY; the import boundary
    accepts any string id. F1: application is now canonical-winner gated — per-batch traversal
    absorbs tombstones into a bounded per-id winner map (capacity
    TOMBSTONE_REPLAY_MAX_RECORDS_PER_CYCLE), one additional bounded veto scan per batch records
    the batch ids' maximum live timestamps, and a tombstone applies only when greater-or-equal
    (ties prefer deletion, matching newer_record); vetoed ids are canonical-loss no-ops; batch
    state persists in the traversal sidecar; the veto runs uniformly in every generation and is
    structurally a no-op on exporter-canonical trusted generations; the pass stays sort-free,
    so the R34 retirement stands. F2: tombstone id is validated as a UUID at all three
    boundaries — import fails visibly pre-transaction (MemoryImportError), exporter
    canonicalization fails visibly (MemoryExportError, no trust stamp), and the replay resolves

    (newer_record has no future-timestamp guard; tombstone admission gates only id shape and
