    deleted_at)). F1: the marker is deleted outright — the shared admission helper stamps
    utc_now() exactly as import does, the recorded maximum is always a concrete timestamp
    persisted in batch state, and the ordinary newer_record ordering resolves past- and
    future-dated tombstones identically to import; strictly less mechanism than R49. The
    both-missing acceptance arm became past- and future-dated tombstone parity arms. F2: the
    exporter value axis became canonicalization rather than rejection — project_id normalizes
    to a canonical UUID string or null (invalid or oversized) at the exporter, at import
    admission, and at the replay winner-map parse; canonical five-field lines are bounded by
    construction and the size check survives as a defensive invariant; the R44 arm now proves
    the explicit-export remediation end-to-end for the formerly wedged over-ceiling input,
    the roster citation of test_export_rejects_overcap_canonical_tombstone was replaced, and
    the over-ceiling arm distinguishes tombstone from non-tombstone survivors of the rewrite.
    Both required_resolutions followed; F1 deviates only by removing the marker entirely
    instead of retaining a marker-shaped special case — same semantics, less mechanism.
    Mid-round scope check from Josh: tombstones confirmed active code (32d8582ed, #16740)
    under LWW merge semantics; the missing lifecycle policy (TTL/GC) is filed as research
    task #18597, out of scope for this plan. Validation passed. Finding count trajectory:

**Round 51** `kind: verification`

- reviewer_run: 257e662e-24ee-4cb3-a19a-33d8500b1f52
- reviewer_session: 49fc6484-be00-4540-820c-5db03875c4ec
- verdict: needs_review
- findings:
  - overceiling-invalid-id-tombstone-remediation-wedge / blocking / R50's remediation claim
    ("explicit export genuinely recovers every input") contradicted the still-current R48 F2
    export rule (fail visibly, no write, no trust stamp, on any invalid-UUID tombstone id):
    a `_deleted: true` line with an invalid id above TOMBSTONE_REPLAY_MAX_FOREIGN_LINE_BYTES
    cannot be classified by bounded replay, and explicit export — the one named remediation —

    starving every later valid tombstone. Fix-induced defect in R50's claim, not a re-raise
    of R48's under-ceiling invalid-id handling.
- resolution_notes: Interactive round (Josh present). Claims verified before applying:
    export_locked re-parses the live file line-by-line via json.loads with no line ceiling
    (src/gobby/sync/memories.py:669-778); memories.id is UUID-typed and apply_tombstone binds
    the id into that predicate (verified R48/R50). Fix by provability, deviating from the
    required_resolution's over-ceiling scope with justification: the exporter now omits any
    vacuous tombstone (invalid-UUID id — can never name a memories row) as a named, counted
    diagnostic and publishes + trust-stamps, at any line size — one uniform rule instead of a
    size-conditional fork that would invite the same finding on the under-ceiling axis. The
    remediation claim is narrowed from "recovers every input" to "terminates on every input",
    explicitly naming the sole non-mechanical residue (valid-UUID-id tombstone with
    missing/unparseable remaining durable fields — a real delete intent with unknowable
    ordering) as a visible export failure naming line and id, matching replay's
    fail-visibly-without-advance for the same input — closing the deleted_at-axis variant of
    this wedge preemptively. Import's visible rejection preserved per required_resolution.
    Acceptance gains the end-to-end combined-case arm
    (test_replay_advances_after_export_omits_overceiling_invalid_id_tombstone); export test
    renamed to test_export_omits_vacuous_invalid_uuid_tombstone_id. Validation passed.
    Process changes this round per Josh: review wrap-up directed — Round 52 runs as the FINAL
    round on a fan-out harness (subagents per boundary contract) with review lessons recorded

    shrink accounting — the guard's baseline excludes exactly the counted vacuous
    invalid-UUID-id tombstone omissions, so proven-vacuous removals never trip it while
    every other canonical-count shrink (dedup collapse, record loss) still raises
    MemoryExportError without force; the 2.1.25 commitment is qualified accordingly; the
    combined-case arm now runs with default force=false against a corpus with no unrelated
    DB records masking the count drop, asserting publication, trust stamp, and subsequent
    replay of the later valid tombstone; companion arm
